For 20 advance chapters: patreon.com/michaeltranslates

# 457: Prophetic Dream

February 21st, Tuesday, Cloudy.

Hoenn, Rustboro City.

"Garrr?"

Having arrived at a new place, Garganacl's eyes flickered with curiosity, caution, and unease—feelings it failed to fully mask beneath its usual imposing aura.

"Aiiy~~"

The ever-gentle Indeedee sister, who could sense the emotions of Pokémon, was now sporting a newly purchased pink bow on her head. She smiled warmly and offered the newcomer a freshly baked cake as a gesture of comfort.

Her superb baking skills, combined with Alcremie's rich cream topping, made the cake look incredibly tempting.

"Garrr?"

Garganacl swallowed a bit of saliva, pointing at the cake, then at itself.

This… This is for me?

"Aiiy~"

The Indeedee sister nodded gently.

"Garrr!"

Garganacl was deeply moved. After taking a bite, it was instantly won over by the cake's taste. How could such an amazing Pokémon like Indeedee exist in this world?!

Its admiration was written all over its face.

Meanwhile, the Indeedee brother shot it a glance before silently handing another cake—prepared as a welcome gift—to Jigglypuff.

"Puri!"

Jigglypuff, however, wasn't as easy to win over as Garganacl. It turned its head away with a huff, refusing to immediately accept the offering. Yet, the way its eyes kept sneaking glances at the cake completely betrayed its true feelings.

"Sis, I'll leave 'Salt King' in your care from now on."

Lying on a lounge chair, flipping through a book while resting, Johan chuckled.

"Aiiy~!" The Indeedee sister responded sweetly.

"Garrr?"

Garganacl tilted its head in confusion before quickly growing excited.

Having such a gentle Indeedee as its caretaker? This was too good to be true!

It failed to notice the sympathetic gazes from the Indeedee brother, Alcremie, and Gengar.

Johan smirked but didn't bother explaining. In a few days, Garganacl would understand his well-intentioned reasoning.

If it wanted to maintain its act, truly becoming a "Salt King" was the best option.

His gaze swept across the courtyard.

With Garganacl's arrival and both Dragonite and Urshifu having evolved, the yard… was starting to feel cramped.

"Maybe I should buy the land behind the house?" Johan pondered.

That way, he could plant more flowers and greenery.

He could also expand the garden for his Ice Radish and Black Radish crops…

Perhaps even plant some berries.

Not necessarily to save money, but at the very least, the Pokémon would have a larger space to roam.

For buying land, the best option was to consult Devon Corporation—he could ask Steven about it later.

"Since our 'Salt King' is here, let's have a salt-baked feast today!"

After roughing it out in the wild for the past few days, and with the food on the cruise ship and at Professor Elm's lab being just average, Johan was eager to test Garganacl's capabilities.

"Garrr?"

The warmth from Indeedee's kindness faded as Garganacl suddenly had a bad feeling.

Had it come to the wrong place?

A shiver ran down its back. Slowly turning its head, it locked eyes with Larvitar—who was staring at it with intense anticipation.

"Garrr!ヾ(▼▼;)"

Garganacl stiffened, trying to put on a serious expression.

But Larvitar wasn't fazed. Instead, it swallowed hard and continued to gaze at Garganacl, eyes filled with eagerness.

"Garrr(﹏)"

With a resigned sigh, Garganacl reached into its rocky body, pulled out a chunk of pure white rock covered in salt, and stuffed it into Larvitar's mouth.

"Yoji!"

Larvitar was overjoyed, immediately plopping down on the ground and chomping away happily.

Garganacl: (﹏)σ

…Why is its taste preference so extreme?!

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar eagerly raised its hand, volunteering to go buy ingredients for the feast.

But Johan ignored it outright.

Letting Gengar do the grocery shopping?

They'd end up with half the supermarket in their house.

This kind of task was best left to Indeedee sister—much more reliable.

Johan even had an internal "reliability ranking" for his Pokémon.

Top Tier: Indeedee siblings, Alcremie, Ninetales.

Mid Tier (-1): Tinkaton, Dragonite, Urshifu, Charcadet—Pokémon that only occasionally went out of control.

Lower Tier (-2): Larvitar—who had already demonstrated exceptional talent in chaos.

Bottom Tier (-10): Gengar—pure chaos incarnate.

Yes. This ranking was perfectly reasonable.

"By the way, where's Marshadow?" Johan suddenly realized a certain little Pokémon was missing.

Ever since returning last time, Marshadow had stopped following Johan as his "shadow guide" and had instead taken on a new role—"Radish Field Guardian."

It was frequently seen patrolling the Black Radish patch, wielding a small wooden stick to fend off any "thieves."

"Oowoo~~"

The ever-graceful Ninetales, resting beside Johan, lifted its tail and pointed under the camping table.

There—

"Puri?!"

Jigglypuff raised its cream-covered face from inside a cake, looking utterly surprised and innocent.

Next to it, hidden in the shadows, Marshadow was standing with its small wooden stick, watching intently.

"Puri!! (≧皿≦)"

Johan chuckled as he glanced around the courtyard.

This little family of his… was quite large now.

His Pokémon were roughly divided into:

Main Battle Team: Gengar, Ninetales, Tinkaton, Dragonite, Urshifu, Charcadet, and Larvitar—seven in total.

Support Pokémon: Currently just Rotom—but a highly versatile one.

It handled everything: crafting Pokéblocks, collecting reports, navigating, cleaning, and even cooking.

Truly, an essential Pokémon for daily life.

Café Employees: Indeedee siblings, Alcremie, and the newly arrived Garganacl—

…who was temporarily just a decorative statue.

Freeloaders: Marshadow and Jigglypuff.

They might leave someday… or they might just keep mooching forever.

Marshadow had at least taken up the role of Radish Field Guardian, but as for Jigglypuff…

Should he open a 'Pokémon Daycare'?

Ding!!

"Food's ready, Roto!"

Rotom, acting as a portable oven, cheerfully announced.

As the oven door swung open, the rich aroma of salt-baked dishes filled the air.

Johan snapped back to reality, glancing at the neatly arranged, salt-crusted dishes before him.

"Charcadet, we're good to go."

"Goka~"

Charcadet, who had been meditating near the grill, nodded and extinguished its flames.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar cupped its hands around its mouth and shouted—

Dinner time!!

In an instant—

The Pokémon who had been playing, training, and hiding across the yard all rushed to the new stone dining table.

A table that had just been crafted by Garganacl and Tinkaton.

As for the bite marks on the edges… those could be ignored.

Ninetales gently tied a white napkin around Larvitar's neck, following Johan's instructions to keep it from biting the table.

The Indeedee siblings distributed Pokéblocks—one per Pokémon, no fighting allowed.

After all, regular food alone wouldn't be enough to keep their appetites satisfied. Pokéblocks were a must-have staple.

Meanwhile, Gengar eagerly cracked open the salt crusts, peeling back layers of foil to reveal steaming-hot dishes.

Salt-Baked Chicken, Salt-Baked Shrimp, Salt-Baked Clams, Salt-Baked Fish…

The normal menu items.

Then there were the "specialty" dishes that no one wanted to mention—Salt-Baked Venom, Salt-Baked Minerals, Salt-Baked Batteries…

A true Salt-Baked Feast!

Tears welled up in Garganacl's eyes.

It had a bad feeling.

If news of this meal spread… wouldn't its entire species be in danger?!

All the Pokémon eagerly turned to Johan.

Johan smiled.

"Let's raise our glasses! A toast to Jigglypuff and our 'Salt King'!"

Clink!

Glasses of Berry Juice, Moomoo Milk, and sodas were downed in one gulp.

"Let's eat!"

Laughter filled the air in one corner of Rustboro City.

March 6th, Friday, Light Rain.

Rustboro Pokémon Academy, Pokémon Psychology Consultation Room.

"Thank you, Professor!"

A female student dressed in Rustboro Pokémon Academy's uniform bowed politely to Johan, her eyes filled with admiration.

Johan smiled. "No need to thank me. Just remember—next time you watch a horror movie, don't bring your Spoink along."

The girl blushed, nodding quickly. "O-okay!"

As she left, Johan stretched his body, letting out a small yawn.

"Time to check on the little ones' training."

He stood up and walked toward the door.

"Johan?"

The moment he stepped outside, he heard a familiar voice.

Turning his head, he spotted Professor Cozmo, looking slightly travel-worn, just as he was about to enter the room.

"Professor Cozmo? Long time no see."

"Hahaha—yeah, I've been busy lately," Cozmo said, scratching his head with a sheepish grin.

Johan's eyes flickered with curiosity.

"A big meteorite?"

Professor Cozmo blinked, looking puzzled. "No, nothing like that."

Johan exhaled slightly in relief.

Good. No big meteorites meant no big problems.

"I was just asking casually."

Cozmo chuckled. "Nah, nothing major. Just small meteorites here and there. Though… the ones that actually reach the ground are rare."

Of course. Rayquaza eats most of them before they even get a chance to land.

Unless Rayquaza was napping, it was rare for meteorites to actually hit Hoenn.

This made life pretty difficult for meteorite researchers like Professor Cozmo—his work was practically on "Hell Mode."

"If you've got time, let's grab a meal together," Johan suggested.

He figured it would be good to check in on Rayquaza and the "Two Dummies" while keeping an ear on Cozmo's research. If needed, he might have to take precautionary measures.

"I'm just here to pick up something real quick—heading out again soon," Cozmo said apologetically.

Johan waved a hand dismissively. "No worries, next time then."

After all, as long as no big meteorites appeared, there was no rush.

"Alright, sounds good."

—

Sinnoh Region, Celestic Town.

Early spring carried a hint of warmth mixed with lingering cold.

Cynthia nestled comfortably into the soft sofa, her golden hair falling over her face, revealing only the curve of her nose.

Her fingers lazily scrolled through her phone as she skimmed the latest Pokémon League updates.

Her lips pressed together slightly as she read the words—

"Youth Master Invitational Tournament."

Beside her, Caitlin was dressed in a white nightgown, leaning against Cynthia while hugging her knees. Her breathing was soft and rhythmic.

Then suddenly—

Caitlin trembled and shot upright, eyes slightly red, as if she had just woken from a nightmare.

Her voice was shaky, carrying a faint trace of tears as she called out—

"Cynthia…"

Cynthia put her phone down immediately.

Gently pulling Caitlin into her embrace, she stroked her head softly, speaking in a soothing voice—

"What's wrong? Did you have a bad dream?"

"Mm…" Caitlin murmured in response, her voice as small as a whisper.

She buried her face in Cynthia's scent, trying to calm herself down.

"Let me guess… you dreamed about losing the Youth Master Tournament?" Cynthia teased lightly, her fingers running through Caitlin's fluffy hair.

"As if I'd ever lose to him!" Caitlin huffed, scrunching her nose in irritation.

Him.

Cynthia chuckled softly.

She still didn't understand why Caitlin had such a grudge against Johan.

"But yeah… I really did have a nightmare."

"Tell me about it?"

"Mm…"

Caitlin took a deep breath, as if trying to recall the details. Then, in a voice barely above a whisper, she spoke—

"I dreamt… of meteorites raining down like a storm… a lonely being searching for its companion… but its partner seemed to be asleep, unable to awaken…"

She paused for a moment before continuing—

"The god of the sky was provoked… and in its fury, it unleashed its wrath upon the earth… triggering tsunamis and bringing forth endless calamity…"

"Hm?" Cynthia furrowed her brows slightly.

She couldn't help but ask—

"Was that… a prophetic dream?"

"Maybe…" Caitlin mumbled, snuggling deeper into Cynthia's arms as though she was drifting back to sleep.

"Where did it happen?"

But Caitlin didn't seem to hear the question anymore.

Instead, she continued murmuring—

"Ancient grudges awaken from distant lands… shaking the world… Its awakening brings forth a series of chain reactions…

This isn't the end.

This is only… the beginning…"

Cynthia's expression turned grim.

She knew Caitlin's abilities well.

As a powerful psychic, her prophetic dreams were rarely wrong.

If her vision was accurate… then trouble was coming.

One problem after another.

Taking a deep breath, Cynthia steadied herself.

Her gaze became sharp and resolute.

"This is our generation's responsibility."

—

Johan carried Larvitar out of the training room, just in time to hear a familiar voice.

"Professor!"

He turned his head.

And there he was—his student, the handsome, strong, and filthy rich Steven.

"Just finished training? What about Roxanne and Flannery?"

It had been a few days since he last saw them.

Steven nodded, smiling. "They've been training really hard lately."

Since classes had ended, the two walked toward the school gate.

"They're training that hard?"

Johan raised an eyebrow.

Roxanne made sense—she was currently acting as a substitute Gym Leader, so she had to keep up appearances.

But Flannery?

She wasn't due to inherit her Gym just yet, and she wasn't the type to train non-stop.

"Professor, you don't know?"

"Know what?" Johan blinked.

Steven pulled out his phone, swiping the screen a few times before showing Johan the display.

"Youth Master Invitational Tournament."

Johan read the title aloud.

…Huh?

…HUH?!

Steven simply nodded in confirmation.

So that flyer Bertha had seen wasn't just some flashy clickbait.

The Youth Master Invitational Tournament was exactly what it sounded like—a tournament exclusively for young trainers who had reached the Master rank.

Unlike the regional Pokémon League Championships, which only required eight Gym Badges (and had wildly varying skill levels), this was a true high-level competition.

Johan's eye twitched.

This wasn't just another tournament.

This was basically a mini Champion's League.

Look at Steven.

He never cared about the Pokémon League… but for this tournament?

He was definitely planning to participate.

And if Steven was competing…

It meant other top-tier trainers were likely entering as well.

If Lance, Cynthia, Palmer, or even Bertha decided to join…

This was turning into a battle royale for the next generation's strongest trainer.

Nope.

Not interested.

Steven casually added, "The champion gets 50 million Pokédollars and some rare, exclusive battle items—ones you can't buy anywhere."

"How much?"

Johan's voice instantly rose in pitch.

Steven blinked. "50 million Pokédollars. But honestly, the money isn't the point—it's the rare items that are—"

Johan coughed.

50 million Pokédollars, huh…

He'd think about it.

Later.

Right now, it was just an announcement.

"By the way, Professor, are you free this weekend?"

Steven put his phone away, his expression turning even more excited than when talking about the tournament.

"The company's exploration team just detected a rare mineral deposit. Since it's the weekend, why don't we—"

Rare minerals?

No wonder Steven was so excited.

So the real reason for his enthusiasm wasn't the tournament—

It was mining.

Johan glanced at Larvitar, still nibbling on its last piece of ore.

"Yoji! Yoji!"

Larvitar flailed its tiny feet in excitement.

"Where?" Johan asked.

If they could dig up something valuable…

Maybe even a Mega Stone or two…

Steven grinned.

"Slateport City."

# 458: Larvitar: The All-You-Can-Eat Buffet Begins!

March 7th, Saturday.

Hoenn, Slateport City.

A streak of orange-red light rose from the horizon where the sky met the sea, illuminating the sky and painting the ocean in vibrant hues. The dazzling glow was breathtaking.

Slateport City was once the largest city in Hoenn.

Situated on the easternmost side of the Hoenn mainland, it housed the second-largest port in the region, serving as the nearest major harbor to Johto.

Although it didn't have a Gym, Slateport was home to Hoenn's largest Pokémon Contest Hall.

Additionally, it boasted Hoenn TV, the Slateport Department Store, the Slateport Art Museum, and various Pokémon organizations, including the Trainer Fan Club and the Contest Fan Club.

What caught Johan's attention the most was Hoenn's largest Berry Market, where fresh berries could be purchased at significantly lower prices. Many Coordinators and Breeders even chose to settle here because of this.

However, Johan and Steven's destination today was not Slateport City itself.

After arriving in Slateport, the two boarded a Devon Corporation speedboat and set off from the city's port.

Their destination: a small island off the coast.

"Rotom, slow down. We're not in a rush."

Johan, gripping the boat's railing tightly, felt the sea breeze against his face, the water splashing, and the waves undulating. His expression was tense.

"Impossible, Roto! o(>O<;;)σ"

Behind the speedboat, sharp dorsal fins cut through the surface of the water, closely trailing them.

A pack of Sharpedo!

At the back of the boat, Gengar stood grinning, making faces at the Sharpedo.

It even wiggled its rear at them mockingly.

How could the Sharpedo tolerate that?

Originally, they had only been scouting for food, locking onto the speedboat and giving chase.

When they realized they couldn't keep up, they were about to give up.

But Gengar refused to let them leave in peace. Its provocative actions sent the Sharpedo into a rage, making them even more determined to chase the boat.

Johan: "…"

This damn Gengar…

Fortunately—

With Rotom at the helm, the boat was fast enough. Whenever the Sharpedo tried to attack, Gengar would immediately counter with a barrage of blows.

Despite their fury, the Sharpedo had no choice but to retreat in frustration.

What else could they do?

They couldn't catch up, they couldn't win a fight, and they weren't about to just keep watching Gengar taunt them.

By the time the Sharpedo gave up, the boat had already reached the waters near their destination.

"Professor?"

Johan, looking slightly pale, raised a hand to signal Steven that he was fine.

Once they set foot on the island, Johan let out a deep breath and instantly felt much better.

And then—

"Ka-kaah! (;Д｀)"

Gengar tried to back away, but Johan grabbed it and gave it a 'massage of love.'

"Professor can now deal direct damage to Ghost-types?" Steven murmured, rubbing his chin as he watched.

After "educating" Gengar, Johan and Steven finally took in their surroundings.

The island wasn't particularly large.

A dense forest bordered the coastline, while at the center of the island stood a rocky mountain, roughly 200-300 meters tall.

Because there was no space for an airstrip, they had chosen to travel by boat instead.

"I've already uploaded the coordinates to Rotom." Steven said.

Devon Corporation had used high-tech scanners to detect mineral deposits. By uploading the data to Rotom's Pokédex, they could navigate directly to the site.

"Rotom navigation, mission ready!"

Rotom chirped, seamlessly switching to navigation mode.

"Skarmory, Claydol."

Steven released two Poké Balls.

"Skreee!!"

Skarmory flapped its metallic wings, slicing through the air like blades, and soared high into the sky.

Meanwhile, Claydol hovered beside Steven, its eyes glowing faintly with psychic power, scanning the surrounding environment.

Steven had gotten much stronger.

Watching Skarmory take off, Johan nodded in approval.

He recalled the time he once commanded Steven's Skarmory to teach Lance a lesson.

Now, that same Skarmory had far surpassed its former self.

Which made sense.

Back then, Gastly had evolved into Gengar.

If anything, that was the more shocking transformation.

"Professor?"

Steven, about to step into the forest, noticed Johan standing still and called out.

Johan snapped out of his thoughts.

Steven's smooth, practiced actions were enough proof that his years of mining had not been in vain.

—

As they entered the dense jungle, sunlight filtered through the lush canopy, casting dappled shadows on the ground.

A Wurmple clung to a tree, camouflaging itself as part of the bark.

A Caterpie, perched on a branch, took a cautious nibble of a leaf, then quickly looked around nervously.

A Pidgeotto circled above, scanning for prey, while an Ariados meticulously wove its web between the branches.

The moment Johan stepped into the forest, he could feel it—

They were being watched.

"The wild Pokémon here are fairly high level," Johan noted, unfazed.

For an ordinary Trainer, this might be dangerous.

For him and Steven?

Not even remotely threatening.

"That's because this island was previously unrecorded on any maps," Steven explained.

The vast ocean held countless islands, many of which had never been discovered or documented.

Places like Dragonite's Island and Rotom's Island were even more unique, hidden away due to their natural anomalies.

"But this island's biggest value lies in its mineral deposits." Steven added.

In other words—

The Pokémon near the rocky mountain were likely even stronger than those in the forest.

—

It wasn't long before they emerged from the jungle, arriving at the base of the mountain.

"Gurrr~~"

The moment they stepped out, they spotted a group of Graveler and Geodude near a cluster of bushes and scattered rocks, foraging for food.

Further back, a massive Onix lay camouflaged among the stones, basking in the sun.

"Professor, let's get started!"

Now that they had reached the mining site, Steven could hardly contain his excitement.

"Alright!"

Johan nodded.

Technically, this island was still unclaimed land.

However, once Devon Corporation's land purchase request was approved by the League, the island would officially belong to them.

If not for Steven, Johan wouldn't have even known about this place.

So this was essentially a "sneak peek" at free minerals.

Steven, of course, was hunting for rare, valuable stones.

Johan, on the other hand—

He just needed rocks. Any rocks.

—

"Come on out."

Steven released Aggron and Armaldo.

Claydol scanned the area with Psychic, while Aggron and Armaldo began digging.

"You too."

Johan tossed his Poké Balls, summoning Ninetales, Tinkaton, Larvitar, and Gengar, alongside Rotom, who continued navigating.

Tinkaton immediately swung its hammer, using its magnetic field to locate metallic ores.

Rotom emitted golden sparks, using electromagnetic waves to assist in the search.

Gengar scouted ahead, while Ninetales dug with precision.

But even with all that—

None of them were faster than Larvitar.

To Larvitar, this entire place was an 'all-you-can-eat buffet.'

Why waste time digging?

Just eat the rocks straight out of the ground!

"Yoji! Yoji! (^^)"

Buffet time!

The wild Onix, Graveler, and Sandslash all stared blankly at Gengar and the others.

Was this… a raid?

Especially Larvitar, chomping away without hesitation.

Even Johan was stunned.

If someone saw this, they'd think he'd been starving Larvitar for weeks.

—

Suddenly—

Johan's phone buzzed.

He glanced at the screen.

His expression froze.

The message was from someone he hadn't heard from in months—

"Who Am I?"

Mewtwo.

# 459: Deoxys?

Southeast direction, three hundred meters?

Johan turned his head to look.

At first glance, it appeared to be just an ordinary pile of rubble.

Hmm?

Not entirely.

Beneath the rocks, it seemed like something was hiding—some kind of Pokémon.

Trusting Mewtwo and curious about what it had sensed, Johan gave Gengar a look.

The chubby ghost immediately understood and silently floated over.

Rumble—

However, as Gengar approached, whatever was resting beneath the pile of stones woke up. As the rocks tumbled away, a Pokémon emerged into Johan and Gengar's line of sight.

Its body was covered in thick armor resembling a tire, gray in color, with a long, armored trunk and two sharp, tusk-like fangs.

"Donphan, the Armor Pokémon. Ground-type. It is said that once it becomes enraged, it curls into a ball and charges wildly, capable of easily demolishing houses, Loto!"

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar cheerfully greeted it.

But for Donphan, which had been peacefully sunbathing, being suddenly disturbed by a chilling presence was infuriating.

"Mehh!!"

Donphan let out a low growl, its eyes turning red with irritation. It scraped the ground twice with its front foot before lunging forward, rolling aggressively.

Rollout!

At a glance, it really did look like a rolling tire with tusks.

"Ka-kaah—"

Gengar smoothly sidestepped the attack, spreading its hands as if to say, I didn't even do anything this time.

"If it's cranky from lack of sleep, let's put it back to sleep," Johan said.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar grinned mischievously.

This was easy.

Dodging another of Donphan's charges, Gengar rubbed its hands together, forming a dark sphere before hurling it at the rolling Pokémon.

Boom!!

The sudden attack disrupted Donphan's momentum, forcing it to a halt. It shook its head, trying to charge again.

But just as it lowered its head, a ghostly face emerged from its shadow, terrifyingly eerie.

However, before it could fully process the fear, Donphan's eyelids grew heavy. After blinking twice, it couldn't resist the overwhelming drowsiness and collapsed onto the ground with a thud, falling into a deep sleep.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar flashed Johan an "OK" hand sign before diving into the spot Donphan had been resting, searching for the reason Mewtwo had pointed them there.

Johan approached Donphan, gently patting its tough hide—it felt like slapping a thick stone wall.

As a gesture of apology, he placed a handful of Pokéblocks beside it.

"Ka-kaah!"

At that moment, Gengar's head popped out from where it had been digging, calling out to Johan with excitement.

Johan's mind stirred.

A discovery?

But it seemed to be underground.

"Larvitar," Johan called.

"Yoji?"

The small Pokémon, holding a pitch-black mineral chunk and chewing eagerly, looked at Johan in confusion, bits of rock stuck around its mouth.

Realizing what was needed, Larvitar quickly stuffed the last piece of rock into its mouth, chewed hastily, and patted its backside before trotting over.

Where? Where?

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar waved it over.

"Yoji!"

Larvitar nodded vigorously and immediately started digging. In no time, a hole was left in the ground as both Pokémon burrowed deeper.

But before either Pokémon could resurface, a faint green glow emerged from the tunnel.

Huh?

Johan stared in surprise.

The glow grew brighter and closer until a diamond-shaped green light floated out and shot straight into Johan's phone.

"What is this…"

Just as the light vanished into his phone, Johan snapped out of his daze.

That thing… looked oddly familiar.

As he held his phone to check, the screen became blurry, twisting strangely as green light flickered across it, almost like it had been infected by a virus.

But soon, the device suddenly heated up, radiating a strange energy. The distortion on the screen cleared, returning to normal, except for a single line of text that appeared.

Johan's expression turned odd.

His phone was really something else.

No wonder—it was made by the League.

Though he wasn't entirely sure what that green light had been, he had a good guess about why his phone had suddenly heated up.

It was Mewtwo, using Porygon2 as a medium to channel power.

Johan let out a slow breath.

"You're really working hard, huh…"

He lightly ran his fingers over the back of his phone, feeling a mix of admiration and amusement.

This device had already been "baptized" by Mewtwo and Giratina, and now it had mysteriously been "invaded" by another force.

Good thing he hadn't let Rotom inhabit this phone. Otherwise, with Rotom's jumpy nature, it might've passed out from shock.

Refocusing, Johan turned his gaze back to the screen.

[█████]: Friend!

Friend?

What does that mean?

What exactly had Mewtwo sensed?

With uncertainty, Johan sent Mewtwo a message.

[Grain in the Field]: What is this?

But Mewtwo didn't respond. Instead, a new notification appeared:

[Porygon2]: Downloading data… Constructing information model (awaiting input).

A message from Porygon2?

A suspicious update?

Then that earlier message—Who Am I—was that Mewtwo's thoughts or Porygon2's?

Or had it been Mewtwo's subconscious discovery, relayed through Porygon2?

Johan looked up at the sky.

You two sure have a complicated relationship…

"Ka-kaah!"

"Yoji!"

At that moment, Gengar and Larvitar resurfaced, both covered in dirt but looking thrilled.

Larvitar was carrying a rock nearly as big as itself—or maybe even bigger.

The rock looked odd, resembling volcanic slag with numerous tiny holes pockmarking its surface. Under the light, it reflected different colors, indicating a high metal content.

Larvitar was clearly struggling to hold it.

But the most notable feature was the gemstone embedded in the rock—a green, oval-shaped stone the size of a palm.

Its surface shimmered like a polished gem, but its core was dull and lifeless, as if missing something crucial.

Seeing this, Johan's heart skipped a beat.

If he couldn't recognize this, he might as well stop calling himself a Pokémon enthusiast.

This was Deoxys' core crystal!

He remembered the Pokémon: Destiny Deoxys movie, where two Deoxys arrived from space—one with a purple core, the other with a green core.

The green-core Deoxys had gone into hibernation, while the purple-core Deoxys fought Rayquaza in an epic battle.

Though the purple-core Deoxys was eventually defeated, it managed to regenerate after four years.

Later, it even set up an impenetrable barrier, leading to Rayquaza's infamous reputation as the "Eternally Frustrated Dragon."

And the reason for that Deoxys' actions?

It was looking for its missing counterpart—the very same green-core Deoxys Johan had just unearthed.

Then…

The green diamond-shaped light that had entered his phone—it had to be Deoxys' consciousness!

His phone vibrated again.

[█████]: Friend! Friend!

Johan stared at the screen, reading the repeated plea.

The green-core Deoxys was reaching out for help.

After a moment of silence, he let out a deep breath.

"Well, looks like I have another Pokémon to save."

"Friend!"

Johan's phone vibrated repeatedly, displaying the same word over and over.

Steven had also noticed what Larvitar had dug up, his eyes flashing with intrigue.

As a rock enthusiast, Steven had an extensive collection.

But he was certain—this was a rock he had never seen before.

Johan's thoughts stirred, and he smiled.

"What do you think? Interested? You can have it."

His phone vibrated again.

[█████]: Friend!

Steven's eyes lit up momentarily, but he quickly regained his composure and shook his head.

"No, you found this, teacher."

"It's fine. I could sell it to you, you know I've never been particularly interested in rocks," Johan replied.

"Yoji!"

Larvitar whimpered softly, glancing pitifully between Johan and the rock it held. It then hesitated, sticking out its tongue to lightly lick the stone, reluctant to part with it.

Such a fine rock...

It must taste incredible.

[█████]: Friend! Friend!

Johan's phone vibrated incessantly.

Steven laughed heartily. "Teacher, I understand your kindness. But look, Larvitar seems very attached to it—perhaps it sees it as a highly tempting meal."

A meal?

Johan pinched the bridge of his nose.

Eating Deoxys?

Even Rayquaza wouldn't dare.

If Larvitar actually swallowed Deoxys' core crystal, another Deoxys might show up tomorrow and obliterate them all!

"Cha~~"

At that moment, Tinkaton and Ninetales trotted over.

Tinkaton, gripping its massive hammer, circled Larvitar and the rock, scrutinizing it.

It then took out its hammer and lightly tapped both the meteorite and the green gemstone, its eyes gleaming with fascination.

"Cha~ Cha~!"

It turned toward Johan, chirping excitedly.

This metal's flexibility and resilience—perfect for reinforcing Charcadet's armor joints!

Steven chuckled. "Looks like Tinkaton also sees value in this stone."

Johan remained silent, staring at the sky.

But this thing was a hot potato.

It was only a matter of time before the other Deoxys came looking for it.

And he wasn't even sure if this was the same Deoxys from the movie, the manga… or neither.

He glanced down at his phone.

The screen was flooded with messages reading "Friend", as if it was desperate for him to understand its significance.

Yet beneath those repetitive pleas, Johan could sense Deoxys' distress.

He exhaled slowly.

He had helped many Pokémon before—some belonging to trainers, but also plenty of wild ones.

His café even had a notice offering free aid to wild Pokémon in need.

This Deoxys was simply seeking help, just like the others.

"Whoosh—"

Johan let out a long breath.

At the end of the day…

Neither this green-core Deoxys nor the purple-core one had ever acted with malice.

They were only looking for each other.

And while their power was terrifying, power without hostility could be an asset.

Even if…

Even if this really was the same Deoxys from the movie and Rayquaza eventually came knocking…

Well, Johan could always sit down and have a nice chat with the sky dragon.

Maybe discuss strategies for handling incoming meteors.

Or, more realistically, how to deal with the two "idiots"—Groudon and Kyogre—when they inevitably woke up again.

But just to be safe…

"Steven, I think this rock… doesn't quite seem like it belongs to our world. It feels like a meteorite," Johan said, shifting the conversation.

Given Steven's expertise, he immediately agreed.

"Yes, the outer layer is definitely some kind of space-borne meteorite."

"Then why didn't Mossdeep Space Center detect it?" Johan asked.

Steven blinked in surprise before furrowing his brows.

"Normally, any falling meteorites would be registered by the Space Center, and a specialized team would recover them."

After a brief pause, he added,

"But there are exceptions. And… for certain reasons, the Space Center's monitoring system has been known to have… occasional lapses."

Certain reasons?

Rayquaza capture project?

Johan narrowed his eyes slightly, adjusting his monocle.

"We're not far from Mossdeep. Should we go check it out?"

# 460: Steven, Call for Reinforcements!

Viridian City, Kanto—Team Rocket Base.

Buzzz—

Inside a secret underground laboratory belonging to Team Rocket, red warning lights flashed as the power fluctuated chaotically, illuminating the darkened research facility.

Yet, despite the disturbance, the researchers in white lab coats remained unfazed—calm, even.

Small matter.

Lab explosions were a regular occurrence. A mere power outage? Child's play.

Besides—

Mewtwo's psychic energy fluctuations were unstable, suggesting it was close to waking up, though not fully conscious yet.

"It's Porygon2."

A researcher holding a tablet pinpointed the source of the anomaly.

"Porygon2?"

The head of the lab looked slightly surprised upon hearing the answer.

Since when… did Porygon2 have the ability to cause "chaos" like Mewtwo?

"Run a recovery protocol," the head researcher ordered in a calm tone.

"Well… that might not be possible."

"Hm?"

"Porygon2… isn't accepting commands right now."

Not accepting commands?

Unlike Mewtwo—whose genetic origins remained shrouded in mystery—Porygon had been meticulously designed and programmed with extensive resources dedicated to its development, from its genetic structure to its behavior and personality.

Calling it a Pokémon was a bit of a stretch.

It was more like a highly advanced AI—a multifunctional, mobile supercomputer.

Though, due to certain… special circumstances, Porygon2 had developed a degree of intelligence.

But even so, that intelligence was supposed to be limited.

"What about a system reset?"

"Uh…" The researcher hesitated before turning the screen of their tablet toward the lab director.

Displayed on the screen—

The usual control interfaces had completely disappeared, replaced by an image of Porygon2…

Surrounded by a swarm of Gastly.

The lab director: "…"

—

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar shoved the last chunk of ore into its belly and patted its now-round stomach, feeling satisfyingly full.

Sure, it had access to a personal dimensional storage, but carrying all this ore was still quite a task.

Then again…

Each of these mineral chunks was worth at least several hundred Pokédollars. Every piece it stuffed inside felt like shoveling in cash—Gengar was very pleased.

Who didn't love free money?

"Yoji~~"

Larvitar, its belly just as round, trailed closely behind Gengar, never leaving its side.

At this moment, Gengar was nothing less than a walking food supply, and Larvitar had to guard it with its life.

Though they had "accidentally" unearthed Deoxys' green core crystal, Johan wasn't in a hurry to head to Mossdeep Space Center just yet.

Instead, they took their time mining, stocking up on supplies for Larvitar before finally preparing to leave.

Mossdeep City was located on an island in eastern Hoenn, much like Sootopolis City and Pacifidlog Town.

And since it wasn't far from Slateport City, it was even closer to Johan and Steven's current location.

The only obstacle was crossing the sea.

Johan firmly held Gengar in place.

Without its interference, Rotom had a much easier time steering the speedboat, eliminating the nauseating, weightless swaying.

Johan held his phone while sitting near the meteorite and Deoxys' green core crystal.

Meanwhile, Tinkaton was attempting to separate the core from the meteorite, hammering away with clink-clank sounds.

Johan focused his Aura, trying to communicate with Deoxys.

But no matter how much he attempted, the core remained unresponsive.

All his phone displayed was—

"Friend, friend."

…Okay.

As long as it didn't add "Friend, delicious", this was still manageable.

Whoosh—

The ocean surged, waves rolling suddenly.

The commotion was noticeable but not particularly violent.

"Yoji! (Д)σ"

Larvitar leaned against the boat's railing, eyes wide, pointing at the massive figure rising from the sea.

Such… a big fish!

As the surface of the ocean broke apart, a colossal Wailord emerged, creating rolling waves.

From Johan's vantage point, it looked like a giant submarine surfacing from the depths.

Before Dynamax Pokémon were discovered, Wailord had been the largest non-Legendary Pokémon.

Following its appearance, several Wailmer, bobbing like balloons, surfaced around it.

"Wailord and Wailmer are a well-known sight in the waters around Mossdeep," Steven explained casually.

Pssshh—

Wailord noticed Johan's speedboat and sprayed a burst of water from its spout.

The water arched high before cascading down in shimmering droplets.

The Wailmer followed suit, spraying smaller fountains.

As the sunlight hit the falling droplets, a vibrant rainbow formed in midair.

There was no hostility—this was simply Wailord and Wailmer's way of greeting them.

"Yoji…"

Larvitar gulped.

Such big fish…

It turned to Johan.

"Yoji?"

How about salt-grilled?

Johan: "??"

Wailord: "??"

Wailmer: "??"

Glug-glug—

Wailord and its pod promptly dove back into the ocean.

They were out.

"Ka-kaah~!"

Still being held in place by Johan, Gengar gave Larvitar a thumbs-up.

Great minds think alike!

…Oh no.

Larvitar was starting to pick up Gengar's bad habits.

"Your Pokémon never change, do they, Teacher?" Steven remarked with a chuckle.

Johan: "…"

—

Twinleaf Town, Sinnoh—Godfrey Estate.

Gentle music floated through the garden.

Darkrai lay on the grass, having recently learned to enjoy the warmth of sunlight.

Suddenly—

It opened its eyes. Black-and-white wisps of shadow energy flickered around its body.

"Darkrai?"

Alice, who had been playing a grass flute surrounded by Pokémon, looked at it in surprise.

"He needs my help," Darkrai said solemnly.

Alice's expression grew serious.

"Then… be careful."

Darkrai's eyes gleamed faintly with amusement.

"In Hoenn? As long as it's not Rayquaza, I can handle it."

—

Mossdeep City Gym Training Grounds.

Two young children—clad in nearly identical robes resembling those of monks—simultaneously opened their eyes.

Floating beside them were Solrock and Lunatone.

They were Tate and Liza, the twin Gym Leaders of Mossdeep.

"There's trouble," Liza suddenly said.

Tate nodded. "Not far now."

"But… it seems…"

"…Someone strong is coming, too."

"If the sky collapses…"

"…The tall ones will hold it up."

Their conversation flowed smoothly, almost as if they shared a single mind.

"Should we go meet them?" Tate asked.

But before the words had fully left his mouth, they both responded simultaneously—

"No need. They'll come to us."

Then, they closed their eyes again.

Faintly—

A soft psychic energy flickered around them.

—

Mossdeep Space Center.

Originally part of Greater Mauville Holdings, the facility had been acquired by Devon Corporation after the former went bankrupt.

Now, it was a key asset of Devon.

Inside the space center's lobby, Gengar curiously examined the advanced technology, glancing around.

"Johan, Steven."

A familiar voice greeted them—Professor Cozmo.

In addition to his role at Rustboro Pokémon Academy, he held an important position at Mossdeep Space Center.

Since Johan and Steven had requested a visit, he was the one assigned to receive them.

"Sorry for dropping by unannounced," Johan said apologetically. Steven nodded in agreement.

"No, no, it's fine." Professor Cozmo waved it off with a laugh. "Honestly, your timing is great. Now I have an excuse to take a break. Haha—"

"You sound like work here is tough," Johan joked.

"You have no idea."

Professor Cozmo sighed, and he and Johan simultaneously turned to glance at Steven.

Steven suddenly looked embarrassed.

It sounded like they were blaming Devon Corporation for overworking him.

—

Johan's eyes widened at the screen.

Never mind the reports…

"Steven," he said, voice dead serious.

"Call for reinforcements."

Steven immediately picked up on Johan's shift in demeanor.

"…What did you see?"

Johan's expression remained serious as he fixed his gaze on the massive screen.

Displayed on it was an image taken from a space observation station—a rare, radiant purple aurora.

At first glance, it seemed like a breathtaking celestial phenomenon.

But Johan knew better.

"Professor Cozmo," he said, his voice steady. "Where was this aurora observed?"

Professor Cozmo frowned slightly but quickly pulled up additional data on a nearby terminal.

"This was recorded by one of our external observatories in Hoenn," he explained. "More specifically, over Fallarbor Town."

Fallarbor Town?

Johan's fingers tightened slightly around his phone.

That wasn't just any aurora.

That was Deoxys' energy signature.

The purple-core Deoxys.

If the green-core Deoxys—the consciousness that had entered his phone—was here…

Then the other one was already looking for it.

Johan turned to Steven. "I need you to contact someone for me."

"Who?"

Johan exhaled.

"If we're dealing with Deoxys, we may need someone who can stand on equal footing with it. Someone who's fought it before."

Steven's eyes widened slightly as realization dawned on him.

"You mean…?"

Johan nodded.

"Call Rayquaza."

Steven: "…"

Professor Cozmo: "…"

Even Gengar and Larvitar, who had been distracted by the space center's displays, turned their heads in stunned silence.

"Ka-kaah?"

"Yoji?"

Steven rubbed his temples. "Johan, you make it sound so easy."

Johan shrugged. "Well, you are Hoenn's former Champion. If anyone can get a direct line to Sky Pillar, it's you."

Steven sighed. "You do realize that Rayquaza doesn't exactly pick up the phone and say, 'Hello, how can I help you?'"

"But someone can reach Rayquaza," Johan countered. "Someone who communicates with it."

Steven's face darkened. "Wallace…"

Johan nodded. "Exactly. Get in touch with Wallace. Now."

As much as Steven disliked dealing with Wallace's dramatic tendencies, he understood the urgency.

He pulled out his own phone and stepped aside, making the call.

Johan, meanwhile, turned back to Professor Cozmo.

"Do we have any real-time monitoring on Fallarbor's skies?"

Professor Cozmo hesitated before shaking his head. "The aurora readings are recorded, but we don't have constant surveillance over that area. However… if you want more information, there's someone else you should talk to."

Johan raised an eyebrow. "Who?"

"Raizoh Cozmo."

Johan's gaze sharpened.

Raizoh Cozmo—Professor Cozmo's father.

A legendary scientist known for his research into meteorites, Raizoh had devoted his life to studying the connection between space and Pokémon.

If anyone in Hoenn understood what was happening, it would be him.

"Where is he now?" Johan asked.

Professor Cozmo sighed. "He's in Meteor Falls."

Meteor Falls—a place where countless meteorites had landed over the ages.

And the same place where Team Magma and Team Aqua had once fought over a meteor fragment containing unknown cosmic energy.

Johan's instincts flared.

Something was pulling all these events together.

Deoxys… Rayquaza… Meteor Falls…

This was no coincidence.

At that moment, Steven returned from his call. His expression was unreadable.

"What did Wallace say?" Johan asked.

Steven sighed. "He said… he'll take care of it."

Johan's eyes narrowed. "Does that mean—?"

"He's already on his way to Sky Pillar," Steven confirmed.

Johan exhaled slowly.

Good.

Now, there was only one thing left to do.

"Let's move," he said. "We're going to Meteor Falls."

Powerstones?

# 461: The Visitor from the Sky—Deoxys

Mossdeep City, a secret base.

"F-Fire Admin, bad news! The League police force and Officer Jenny from Mossdeep City have suddenly mobilized!"

A Team Magma grunt, clad in their signature uniform, rushed in with an urgent report.

"Hmm?"

A tall, burly man in the same uniform—Fire Admin Tabitha—furrowed his brows and said in a deep voice,

"Stay calm!"

He stood up and walked to a large communication device, flipping the switch to establish contact.

"Has something happened? What's their objective?"

As he spoke, the communicator connected.

"I-I don't know!"

Despite the sudden crisis, Tabitha's composed demeanor helped the grunt gradually calm down. "We're still unclear on the details!"

At the same time, the connection was completed, and the projection of Maxie, the leader of Team Magma, appeared on the large screen.

He sat at his office desk, hands folded before his face, his tone indifferent.

"Tabitha, what is it?"

Tabitha gave a slight bow. "Boss, the League police in Mossdeep City have suddenly gone active. The reason is unknown. Should we cancel our plan to raid the Mossdeep Space Center?"

Maxie's eyes, hidden behind his glasses, narrowed slightly.

"Only a few people know about the plan—there's no chance of a leak. It seems some other event has caused chaos. This is a good opportunity."

As long as the League wasn't targeting them, the greater the chaos, the better for Team Magma.

"The plan remains unchanged," Maxie declared firmly. But after a brief pause, he added,

"However, investigate the cause of this disturbance. If necessary… give it a little 'push.'"

"Understood," Tabitha nodded.

"I'll send—"

Before Maxie could finish his sentence, the communication was abruptly cut off.

The entire base had suddenly lost power without warning.

Panic spread within the base, but Tabitha remained relatively calm. He had anticipated the possibility of disruption—he just hadn't expected it to happen so soon.

While quickly working to restore order, he turned to the grunt and commanded,

"Tell our operative to be ready. The moment I give the signal, move immediately!"

"Yes, sir!"

—

Mossdeep City—Steven's house.

After reporting the possible crisis to the League, Steven and Johan returned to Steven's home in the city.

It wouldn't be an exaggeration to say Steven had a "home" in nearly every town in Hoenn.

"Teacher… is it really this serious?" Steven asked, his tone a bit heavy.

He had been standing right next to Johan during his call with Drake, hearing every word.

When Rayquaza was mentioned as a possible arrival, Steven fully grasped the weight of the situation.

But he still couldn't figure out why Rayquaza might descend upon Hoenn.

Johan sighed and spread his hands. "Honestly, I hope I'm just overthinking things."

Perhaps the worst-case scenario wouldn't actually happen.

But the appearance of the purple-core Deoxys was now a certainty.

The question was: When would it arrive in Mossdeep City?

If it sensed the presence of the green-core Deoxys and flew straight here, things would be simple.

Johan would just hand over the core and let them reunite.

But…

In the movie "Destiny Deoxys," the story took place in LaRousse City, yet here, things had already deviated significantly.

More importantly—Gengar and Larvitar had seen the aurora from a recording, not from Mossdeep City itself.

According to the data, the footage was taken north of Autumnleaf Town on a nearby hill.

Meaning—the purple-core Deoxys first appeared near that location.

It was likely on its way to Mossdeep City right now.

But what if Rayquaza intercepted it along the way?

If Rayquaza and Deoxys clashed before reaching Mossdeep, the resulting battle would cause catastrophic destruction to Hoenn.

Because of this risk, the League couldn't focus all their forces on Mossdeep—they had to monitor the surrounding regions as well.

Which meant…

Even though Johan had warned them in advance, it was just a precaution. The League could react a bit faster, but it was impossible to completely control the situation.

Johan had used his authority to have the League police force work overtime, keeping a close watch on Mossdeep and the surrounding areas.

But this wasn't abuse of power.

"Then… that purple aurora was a signal?" Steven asked, trying to connect the dots.

Johan nodded. "It's the other Deoxys trying to send a message to its partner."

Steven blinked. "You studied Deoxys before?"

"Let's just say I know a thing or two." Johan deflected, making his way toward the kitchen.

Mossdeep was an expensive city, and Steven's house was no exception.

Even the kitchen was bigger than Johan's entire bedroom.

"Hey, Steven, you do have food in here, right?" Johan scanned the room but didn't immediately spot the fridge.

"Food?"

Steven paused, then pressed a button on the wall.

An entire section of the wall silently slid open, revealing an enormous fridge.

Johan clicked his tongue.

Once again, he was reminded of the wealth gap.

"You need to eat first to think?" Steven guessed. He had learned to understand Johan's habits over time.

"No." Johan opened the fridge, checking the ingredients. "I just want to have something ready. In case a 'friend' drops by."

"…A 'friend'?"

Johan didn't answer directly. Instead, he said in a calm voice,

"If things really go bad… I'll need that friend to fight for me."

—

Canalave City, Sinnoh.

Sitting aboard a private jet, Flint gazed out the window at the moon, sighing in exasperation.

Seriously?

Overtime? Fine.

Working late? Fine.

But now?

He glanced at the time.

Nearly midnight.

And now, out of nowhere, he was being told to fly from Sinnoh to Hoenn for an "urgent job"?

"Hey, Butler." Flint called softly.

His elderly butler, sitting in front of him, looked up in confusion.

"Yes, young master?"

"Maybe we should… move to another city in the future?"

"Move?" The butler was taken aback.

Wasn't Flint completely in love with Canalave City?

It was surrounded by the ocean, had one of the most famous libraries in the Pokémon world, and was a transportation hub connecting multiple regions.

It was perfect for someone who loved knowledge like Flint.

"Why move all of a sudden?" The butler asked.

Flint sighed, tilting his head toward the window.

"Because… we're too damn close to Hoenn."

—

Sootopolis City, Hoenn.

As the closest major city to Mossdeep, Gym Leader Juan and his apprentice, Wallace, stood near the harbor.

"Wallace, be careful."

As a Gym Leader and a top-tier Contest Coordinator, Juan had already been briefed on the League's emergency alert.

Unlike Juan, who needed to stay in Sootopolis, Wallace—both Steven's friend and Johan's former student—was heading to Mossdeep to assist.

Wallace smiled gracefully. "Don't worry, Master."

Both Steven and Johan were close friends.

If they needed help, Wallace wasn't going to sit back.

Under the moonlight, his poised, elegant figure exuded confidence.

"Besides… Johan is there."

A man rumored to have punched Dialga and Palkia into submission.

Even if those stories were exaggerated, if anyone could handle Rayquaza, it was Johan.

Juan chuckled. "Go and see the world. After all… soon enough, this Gym will be yours."

Wallace nodded. "Of course."

He released a Sharpedo, skillfully leaping onto its back.

Under the moon's glow, he set off toward Mossdeep.

—

Then… the lights in Mossdeep City went out.

For a moment, the entire city was plunged into darkness.

People panicked. But thanks to Johan's early precautions, Officer Jenny swiftly restored order.

And in the night sky above…

A magnificent, pulsating purple aurora shone over the city.

Johan exhaled.

"…It's here."

Johan narrowed his eyes, his gaze locked onto the figure perched atop the Mossdeep Space Center's signal tower.

It was unmistakable—Deoxys.

The purple-core Deoxys had finally arrived.

Its body stood rigid against the backdrop of the aurora it had created, its gem-like core glowing with an eerie radiance.

The scanning lines across its face flickered as it surveyed the city below.

It wasn't looking with normal eyesight.

Deoxys perceived the world through electromagnetic fields, detecting the energy signatures of Pokémon, people, and even technology.

The presence of so many human-made devices and power grids had distorted its vision, making it harder to locate what it was searching for.

That was why it had cut off all electricity in Mossdeep—to eliminate interference.

And now, as it scanned the city once more, it noticed something peculiar.

The humans and Pokémon below were all retreating in the same direction, as if deliberately clearing the area.

Deoxys didn't know why they were doing this…

But it made its search significantly easier.

Yet, not everyone was leaving.

A small group of people was approaching instead—heading directly toward the Space Center.

Deoxys' whip-like arms coiled together, twisting into two solid fists.

Then, with a single bound, it launched itself downward, flying toward the intruders.

It was time to drive them away.

—

Johan's eyes followed Deoxys' sudden movement.

"It's making a move."

He swiftly recalled most of his Pokémon and climbed onto Dragonite's back, securing his flight gear.

He patted Dragonite lightly.

"Go!"

"Draaa!"

Dragonite let out a long cry and shot forward, propelling itself toward the Space Center.

Behind him, Steven tossed a Poké Ball into the air.

"Metagross!"

With a flash of light, his shiny Metagross emerged, hovering beside him.

"Follow them!"

Metagross, its eyes glowing with calculated precision, surged ahead.

—

Meanwhile, on the ground…

The group approaching the Space Center wasn't ordinary civilians.

It was Team Magma.

Taking advantage of the blackout, they had infiltrated the area.

"Admin Tabitha, it's noticed us!"

One of the grunts shouted as Deoxys descended.

Tabitha's expression hardened.

This Pokémon—the one responsible for the citywide blackout—had targeted them first.

"Tch…"

He immediately threw out two Poké Balls.

"Camerupt! Mightyena! Hold it off!"

BOOM!

Camerupt unleashed a pillar of fire, while Mightyena lunged forward, its fangs flashing with dark energy.

CRACK!

But neither attack reached its mark.

An invisible shield—Deoxys' Protect—deflected everything effortlessly.

The alien Pokémon remained unfazed.

Then—

Deoxys' arms snapped apart, shifting back into whip form.

It crossed them over its core, generating a pulsating sphere of energy.

And then—

BOOM!

The Psycho Boost struck with terrifying force, sending Camerupt and Mightyena flying backward.

They crashed to the ground, knocked out instantly.

Tabitha's expression darkened.

This thing was ridiculously strong.

His mind raced.

They needed more time.

They had already pinpointed what they came for.

They just needed to grab it and leave.

With a low growl, he reached into his coat, pulling out a small glass vial filled with a colorless, swirling liquid.

"Let's stir up some more chaos."

He hurled the vial toward Deoxys.

The glass shattered midair—

HISS—

A strange, transparent mist began to spread rapidly, engulfing Deoxys.

The moment it inhaled the substance, Deoxys' entire body trembled.

Its movements faltered.

A sudden, uncontrollable surge of rage erupted in its mind.

Its eyes gleamed with red fury.

—

From above, Johan and Steven watched as Deoxys stiffened midair, its body twitching unnaturally.

Johan's stomach sank.

Something was wrong.

And before he could react—

ROOOAAARRR!

A deafening dragon's roar tore through the skies.

Both Johan and Steven froze.

Slowly, their heads turned upward.

Through the rolling clouds, an enormous green figure coiled and twisted.

The storm-bringer had arrived.

Johan's expression stiffened.

Steven swallowed.

"...Rayquaza."

Johan let out a nervous chuckle.

"Steven… can you handle this?"

Steven: "…???"

Powerstones?

For 20 advance chapters: patreon.com/michaeltranslates

# 462: Everything’s Fine—The Teacher’s Got This

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 12 PM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

"Garrr—!!"

A long, resounding roar shook the night sky, its overwhelming presence pressing down on the entire city.

The Sky Dragon—Rayquaza!

Steven stared at the enormous emerald-green dragon as it tore through the layers of clouds, his mouth slightly open as he murmured,

"It really came…"

Johan had warned him that Rayquaza was likely to appear, drawn by Deoxys' presence.

But no matter how much he had prepared himself, seeing Rayquaza with his own eyes—feeling the sheer pressure of its legendary might—was something else entirely.

Johan half-squinted, sensing Rayquaza's terrifying aura, and gave a slight nod.

Confirmed.

This definitely didn't look like a Pokémon that could be taken down by a bunch of floating cube robots.

If it were that easy, Devon Corporation, the League, and multiple factions wouldn't have needed to join forces to capture it.

"Teacher… what do we do?"

The worst-case scenario had become reality, and Steven instinctively looked to Johan.

When it came to dealing with Legendary Pokémon, his teacher was the expert.

There was no way he was supposed to handle this himself, right?

Sure, he could try, but…

Yeah, probably wouldn't last long.

"Draaa~~"

Johan hadn't even spoken yet, but Dragonite beneath him flapped its wings excitedly—eager for battle.

A Dragon.

A Flying-type.

Everything lined up.

Dragonite's competitive spirit kicked in.

It also carried a fragment of the green orb, which gave it a strange sense of familiarity toward Rayquaza.

Johan gently patted Dragonite's neck, calming it down.

Fighting Rayquaza head-on wasn't the best option.

At least, not right now.

Maybe…

They could talk.

As Rayquaza drew closer, its overwhelming aura intensifying, Johan knew they had to act quickly.

But just as he was about to make a move—

Whoosh!

A blur streaked upward, cutting through the air at incredible speed—facing Rayquaza head-on without backing down.

Deoxys!

Johan's eyes flashed, and he immediately activated his Aura powers.

Aura reaches into the heart!

"Deoxys, your companion—"

Johan was ready to hand over the green-core crystal to Deoxys right now.

He just wanted it to leave peacefully.

If it wanted to fight, it could do so in space.

But to Johan's shock—

When his Aura reached out to Deoxys…

All he felt was rage!

Intense, burning fury!

And the pressure rolling off Deoxys was violent and chaotic.

It rejected Johan's Aura completely.

Johan's expression darkened.

What happened to Deoxys?!

Earlier, it had seemed composed—it simply stood atop the Space Center, scanning the city.

Johan had been confident he could communicate with it through Aura.

As long as he explained the situation and handed over the green core, the problem would be solved.

But now—

"Something's wrong with Deoxys…" Steven also noticed the shift.

He frowned, then suddenly froze.

"Teacher… this feels familiar."

Johan's expression tightened.

More than familiar.

This was exactly like the 'rampaging Pokémon' he had helped Drake cure.

Deoxys… was in a berserk state!

But how?!

Just moments ago, it was fine!

"Garrr!!"

Rayquaza didn't care whether Deoxys was berserk or not.

If anything, Deoxys' aggression only made it more eager to attack.

A golden glow pulsed along Rayquaza's body, gathering into its gaping maw.

Dragon Pulse!

VMMM—

Deoxys' eyes flashed crimson.

It didn't back down.

Its slender form shifted—bulking up into a heavily-armored stance.

A protective barrier flared to life around it.

Defense Forme!

"You cosmic Pokémon sure love shape-shifting, huh…" Johan muttered under his breath.

He quickly signaled Dragonite and Steven to move away—avoiding the attack's direct path.

BOOM!!

Rayquaza's Dragon Pulse smashed into Deoxys.

But…

It didn't break through.

Deoxys was pushed back, but it wasn't injured.

Or rather—

Rayquaza's attack hadn't even touched its actual body.

Rayquaza's golden eyes narrowed slightly, a flicker of thought passing through them.

Deoxys was behaving strangely.

Rayquaza had just wanted a warm-up fight—nothing serious.

But this…

Something wasn't right.

VMMM—

Deoxys' limbs twisted, its arms separating into four whip-like appendages, coiling together in front of its chest.

A brilliantly-colored energy sphere formed between them.

Psycho Boost!

Deoxys fired directly at Rayquaza!

"Garrr!!"

Rayquaza let out a roar, its long serpentine body twisting midair, dodging effortlessly.

BOOM!!

Psycho Boost exploded behind it, sending shockwaves through the sky.

Johan's eyes flickered.

The attack was far stronger than before.

Rayquaza snapped its tail, showing no intention of retreating.

It rushed forward!

Deoxys shifted form again—its body thinning out, its defense dropping sharply, but its speed skyrocketing.

Speed Forme!

It matched Rayquaza's movements perfectly.

From below, Johan and Steven watched the aerial battle unfold—

A series of thunderous clashes, shaking the skies.

"Deoxys is… strong," Steven murmured.

He hadn't expected it to go head-to-head with Rayquaza like this.

Johan glanced at him, lips pressed together.

He didn't explain.

Because…

Deoxys is a walking cheat code.

It could freely switch between its four forms—each with completely different stats.

A normal Deoxys had a base stat total of 600.

But if it switched forms mid-battle—

Attack Forme: 180 in both Attack & Special Attack. Defense Forme: 160 in both Defense & Special Defense. Speed Forme: 180 in Speed.

If Deoxys could freely transition between all of them, its 'real' stat total would be 910.

That was just below Eternamax Eternatus (1125).

Absolutely broken.

And now, it was in a berserk state, meaning its power was even higher than normal.

This fight… was spiraling out of control.

—

Beyond Mossdeep, a private jet trembled violently in the air.

Flint, staring out the window at Rayquaza and Deoxys clashing, felt his soul leave his body.

"WHAT THE F—!!"

The shock was so great, he started cursing.

"Ahem, Young Master," his butler coughed. "Language."

Flint glared at him.

Can you blame me?!

Johan had called him at midnight to rush over because of a "big problem."

But he didn't say the problem was RAYQUAZA!!

And that it was FIGHTING DEOXYS!!

"Wasn't I supposed to avoid this mess after Twinleaf?! Why am I getting dragged into this again?!"

Flint felt like crying.

Overtime? Fine.

Late-night shifts? Fine.

But risking my life for this?!

Flint sighed, opening the jet door.

He tossed out a Poké Ball, summoning Bronzong.

Adjusting his sunglasses against the fierce wind, he jumped.

If he ditched Johan now, he'd definitely be blacklisted for life.

And worse—

He'd probably get dragged into an even bigger mess later.

—

Meanwhile, Johan exhaled slowly, gripping the green-core crystal.

He had one hour.

One hour to fix Deoxys.

Or…

Rayquaza would lose interest in playing around.

And then…

Hoenn would burn.

"Steven," Johan said, eyes sharp.

"We're stopping Deoxys. Now."

Steven nodded, his expression serious.

They couldn't afford to hesitate.

"Metagross!"

Steven's shiny Metagross landed heavily beside him, its red eyes gleaming with battle-readiness.

Johan turned toward his own team.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar grinned, eyes flickering with mischief.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales let out a soft cry, its elegant fur shimmering under the dim city lights.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton twirled its massive hammer, eager to strike.

"Goka!"

Charcadet clenched its fists, flames licking at its armor.

"Ku!"

Kubfu took a fighting stance, ready to leap into action.

Then—

A familiar shadowy mist began swirling beside Johan.

"Finally here?"

The black-and-white fog condensed, revealing a tall, ghostly figure.

Darkrai.

It crossed its arms, gazing at Johan with an exasperated expression.

"You… really can't stay out of trouble, can you?"

Johan chuckled. "Wouldn't be me if I did."

Darkrai sighed. "Fine. What's the target?"

Johan pointed at Deoxys.

Darkrai followed his gaze—just in time to see Deoxys slam its tendrils into the ground, sending shockwaves through the streets.

"…You've got to be kidding me."

Johan patted Darkrai on the shoulder.

"Come on, we've fought worse."

Darkrai glared at him.

"WHEN HAVE WE FOUGHT WORSE THAN THIS?!"

But before Johan could answer—

BOOM!

A blinding burst of energy erupted from Deoxys as it shifted back into Attack Forme.

The rampage had begun.

Johan exhaled sharply, taking a step forward.

"Alright."

"Let's end this."

# 463: [Who Am I]: I’m Here to Help You!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 12 PM EST. So I ask that you save you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Darkrai glanced at Johan.

You sure know how to talk, but when it comes to action, I'm always the one taking the hit.

"I got some tasty treats for you," Johan said, using his Aura.

Darkrai shot him another sidelong glance and snorted coldly.

As if I'd be so easily bribed by food.

"I'll throw in ten boxes of Pokéblocks."

BOOM!!

A dark aura erupted around Darkrai, and it raised a hand, forming a twisted black hole before charging straight at the Deoxys that was violently rampaging through Verdanturf Town.

Johan gave a subtle nod.

Feels like twenty boxes of Pokéblocks would be enough to make Darkrai take on Arceus head-to-head.

Sensing the threat, Deoxys instantly shifted into Defense Forme, conjuring a transparent barrier around itself.

"Die!!"

Darkrai's Dark Void expanded infinitely as it neared Deoxys. Having witnessed its previous clash with Rayquaza, Darkrai seemed to have already devised a countermeasure.

CRACK, CRACK, CRACK—

Ripples spread across Deoxys' protective barrier, intricate cracks forming until—

BOOM!

It shattered completely.

In terms of typing, the Psychic-type Deoxys was completely countered by the Dark-type Darkrai!

Deoxys' eyes flared with anger as it swiftly changed forms again. Four whip-like arms spread wide—Attack Forme!

No backing down!

BOOM!!

The two forces clashed violently in midair. The shockwaves bent the city's trees, sending debris flying in all directions.

Amidst the swirling dust and chaos, both Darkrai and Deoxys were blasted backward.

Deoxys stabilized itself in the air, while Darkrai crashed through a building before landing in front of Johan.

The dark mist around it still churned wildly. Though at a disadvantage, it didn't seem injured.

It simply muttered in a deep voice, "Not bad!"

Its deep blue eyes flickered once more as it gathered energy and charged at Deoxys again.

Seeing this, Fitzwilliam turned and sprinted toward the Verdanturf Space Center.

A pure battle of strength wouldn't be enough to take down Deoxys.

They needed to find the source of its rage.

Johan and Steven exchanged a glance.

Determination shone in both their eyes.

"Metagross!"

Steven stepped forward, his Key Stone radiating a dazzling rainbow light. Beside him, his Shiny Metagross responded in kind.

"Mega Evolution!"

HUM—

"Kong-gin!!"

With a mighty roar, Metagross evolved within the enveloping energy of Mega Evolution.

Mega Metagross!

Johan smiled slightly, adjusting his monocle as his own chest radiated a brilliant light.

"Gengar—"

"Mega Evolve!!"

In an instant, the radiant energy of Mega Evolution illuminated the pitch-black city as if it were broad daylight.

The burst of energy was even greater than Steven's Metagross.

Up in the sky, Rayquaza, lazily reclining within the clouds, tilted its head slightly, blinking twice as it observed the Mega Evolution energy enveloping Gengar below.

Teacher truly lives up to his name.

So this is the power of bonds?

Steven's gaze deepened, admiration gleaming in his eyes.

As the rainbow glow faded, Mega Gengar emerged, crimson-eyed, covered in menacing spikes, with a golden eye opening on its forehead. Half of its body lurked within another dimension, revealing a sinister grin toward Deoxys.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Darkness spread beneath it.

Mega Gengar plunged into the shadow, vanishing—

—Only to reappear behind Deoxys!

A hand formed entirely of shadows emerged, silent and swift, touching Deoxys' body.

A chilling sensation spread through Deoxys, freezing it in place just as it was shifting into Speed Forme to evade Darkrai's attack.

Mega Gengar's Ability—Shadow Tag!

At that moment—

Darkrai surged forward with Dark Void, while Mega Gengar formed a massive black sphere from behind.

A pincer attack!

RUMBLE!!

"That's… Johan-sensei! And Steven!"

On the deck of the ship outside Verdanturf Town, Wallace gasped.

"And a mythical Pokémon—Darkrai," Drake murmured, half-squinting.

So the rumors were true—Johan had indeed captured this fearsome Darkrai.

However—

Even with its movement restricted, Deoxys wasn't about to go down without a fight.

Deoxys shifted into Defense Forme once more, crossing its arms over its chest.

A transparent shield formed, blocking Darkrai and Mega Gengar's attacks.

Held in place between two massive Dark Voids.

"Mega Metagross, Meteor Mash!"

"Kong-gin!!"

Four thick, metallic claws gleamed with an intense steel light as they crashed into Deoxys' barrier from the left.

The shield rippled violently.

"Dragonite!"

"Raaa!!"

Excited, Dragonite flapped its wings, surging with a dark red, menacing aura.

Outrage—Activated!

"Extreme Speed!"

Surrounded by spiraling air currents, Dragonite became a blur of white and dark red, like a massive arrow streaking toward Deoxys!

"Garruu!"

Urshifu braced itself, its aura skyrocketing.

It scoffed inwardly.

Fighting in the sky? How pointless. If Deoxys had the guts, it would come down and battle on solid ground!

"Hm?"

Noticing the blue-tinted Dragonite, Rayquaza scratched its head.

Eh?

Why does this feel like I suddenly have an illegitimate child?

BOOM!!

Dragonite slammed into the right side of Deoxys' barrier, creating another set of ripples.

For a moment, four Pokémon surrounded and assaulted Deoxys!

The sheer spectacle made even the Verdanturf Town residents, who had taken refuge, peek out of their windows.

And what they saw would be seared into their memories forever—

In the dim sky, Darkrai, Mega Gengar, Dragonite, and Mega Metagross were going toe-to-toe with Deoxys!

This was the same Deoxys that had clashed with Rayquaza earlier!

Didn't that mean these four Pokémon were on par with Rayquaza?!

"T-That's…"

"That's Johan-sensei and Steven!"

"What kind of monsters are these?!"

Even Wallace couldn't stop his eyelid from twitching. "They're actually holding it back?"

Drake, seeing more than Wallace, spoke in a low tone.

"Deoxys is still maintaining a massive shield against Rayquaza's attacks."

But even so, this was beyond impressive.

He had received intel from the Pokémon League.

Something was clearly wrong with this Deoxys.

"If only Marshadow were here…" Johan muttered, narrowing his eyes at the sky.

Knowing Marshadow, it would have already charged in with a little stick, knocking Deoxys on the head for an instant KO.

Right now, they were just barely keeping it at bay.

But one thing was certain—this Deoxys was unbelievably strong.

No wonder even Rayquaza struggled against it in the movie.

Though, Rayquaza probably wasn't even going all out. If it didn't use Dragon Ascent, was it really fighting seriously?

"Cha!!"

But even without Marshadow, Johan had another plan.

From Dragonite's back, Tinkaton leaped, hammer gleaming like a falling star, enhanced by three stacks of Swords Dance.

Not aiming to shatter Deoxys' shield—

Just to—

BAM!!

A tremendous force slammed Deoxys and its barrier into the ground.

"Oowoo~~"

Snow and frost swirled.

A northern landscape.

"Garruu!"

Urshifu grinned.

Finally, it came down!

It closed its eyes, adjusted its stance, and threw a spear-like punch straight for Deoxys!

Got it! Here's the continuation of the translation:

BOOM!!

A terrifying surge of Psychic energy erupted from Deoxys, spreading out from its body in a vast field that engulfed almost all of Verdanturf Town.

Psychic Terrain!

HUM—

Riding this explosive burst of energy, Deoxys once again formed an energy sphere. Without any specific target, it locked onto the first opponent within its line of sight—Mega Gengar.

"Ka-kaah!"

Mega Gengar shivered, quickly slipping into the dimensional rift, reappearing behind Darkrai in an instant.

A new message appeared on the screen of Johan's phone, glowing faintly in green light:

[Who Am I]: I'm here to help you!

HUM—

A powerful wave of Aura energy surged straight into Deoxys' consciousness.

It tried to resist, but Darkrai, Mega Gengar, Dragonite, and the others all gritted their teeth and pressed the attack, restraining its movements for the moment.

Rage, hatred, resentment…

Countless negative emotions churned within Deoxys' mind, echoes of its past experiences filling the space.

Even with the support of multiple sources, Johan's Aura alone struggled to break through Deoxys' inner barriers to find its true self.

"Gengar!" Johan called out.

In response, Mega Gengar curled its tongue and spat out a gleaming green core gemstone, holding it up before Deoxys.

At the sight of the green gemstone, Deoxys suddenly froze.

Its struggles came to an abrupt halt, and the chaotic emotions within its mind parted just slightly.

That was the opening Johan needed.

"Deoxys!" Johan's voice resonated deep within Deoxys' consciousness.

"Your companion has been searching for you!"

Companion…

Companion!

Above Verdanturf Town, streaks of purple auroras once again lit up the night sky.

Seeing Deoxys' sudden stillness, Johan quickly stepped forward, raising his phone. The screen emitted a soft, green glow.

A message appeared.

[█████]: Friend!

At that moment—

A mysterious, unseen network of data formed, with Porygon2 actively traversing its pathways. Thin, faint threads of light drifted from Deoxys' body into the digital stream.

Johan's Aura flickered in response.

His Key Stone trembled slightly.

This feeling…

At that very moment—

Kanto, Viridian City—Rocket Team Secret Base.

"In-Evolution Light?"

Inside the research lab, the screen displaying Porygon2 suddenly burst with dazzling white light, drawing everyone's attention.

Porygon2 was evolving?

None of them noticed the subtle fluctuations in the incubation chamber.

There, where Mewtwo slumbered, a strange fusion of Psychic energy and Aura flickered faintly.

Simultaneously, thin, invisible white threads, brimming with an overwhelming life force, converged before it.

Suddenly—

Mewtwo's closed eyes glowed red beneath its dark visor.

It lifted a hand.

The floating white strands coalesced into a single, brilliant gemstone.

If Johan had been present, he would have immediately recognized it for what it was—

A Mega Evolution Stone!

Gently rubbing the stone between its fingers, Mewtwo's glowing eyes gradually dimmed.

Silence.

The secret base returned to stillness.
