# 464: Riding My Beloved Rayquaza (BONUS)

"Friend!"

"Where are you?"

Green and purple lights intertwined as the purple-core Deoxys gradually regained consciousness with Johan's Aura and the removal of the Infinite Energy.

Its struggles finally ceased.

"It..."

Steven and Fitzwilliam walked over.

Johan let out a long breath and plopped down in front of Deoxys. As the foreign Aura left his body, he was overcome by an emptiness—a deep fatigue that he couldn't shake.

Buzz, buzz—

The organ in the center of Deoxys' face, which emitted detection waves, flickered twice.

Johan reacted. "Uh... how long do you guys plan on pinning it down?"

Darkrai, Mega Gengar, Dragonite, Mega Metagross, and the others were stacked atop Deoxys like a pile of bricks.

They exchanged glances.

Is it over?

Can we get off now?

Johan's face darkened.

Are they enjoying this too much?

"Get down."

"Ka-kaah~~"

The energy of Mega Evolution peeled away from Gengar like unraveling silk, restoring it to its original form. The same happened to Metagross.

One by one, they climbed off.

Gengar raised a hand to its forehead, pretending to admire the moon. "Wow, the moon is so round tonight."

Dragonite and Urshifu slung their arms around each other's shoulders, pointing at the stars, "One, two, three… connecting the dots."

Tinkaton lightly tapped Darkrai with its hammer.

Darkrai snapped out of it and started whistling along with Tinkaton.

As if none of this had anything to do with them.

Steven's Metagross, Aggron, and the rest were a bit more well-behaved. They stood there awkwardly, unsure of what to do.

"Yoji, what are you doing?"

The little green-shelled Pokémon rushed over, its stubby legs moving quickly as it headed straight for Deoxys.

Hearing Johan's voice, it froze mid-step.

"Y-Yoji..."

Uh, do you like my new shell? Looks good, right?

It even wiggled its little feet for emphasis.

Johan: "..."

Deoxys: "..."

Already exhausted, Johan felt even more drained.

Rustle, rustle—

After taking a moment to recover, Deoxys slowly floated up from the ground.

It picked up the green-core gem representing its companion and looked at Johan.

Johan gave it a slight nod.

Deoxys placed the green-core gem against its chest.

HUM—

A faint glow radiated from its purple-core gem, gradually brightening from within.

At the same time—

The green-core gem, which Johan had previously been unable to probe with his Aura, seemed to respond to Deoxys' energy. A soft, pale green light flickered at its center.

Others had been unable to awaken this Deoxys, but another Deoxys—one of its kind, with a purple core—clearly could.

As it used its own energy to trigger the green-core gem, the barrier covering Verdanturf Town slowly faded away.

The once-darkened city gradually returned to light.

"Is it over?" Wallace let out a deep breath.

Johan-sensei really is amazing. A true master of the heart—he actually managed to calm Deoxys, a Mythical Pokémon of such power.

Even though it felt like his trip here had been pointless, witnessing that battle made it worthwhile.

As long as Hoenn was safe, that was enough.

"No."

Drake wasn't as optimistic.

His eyes were locked on the massive green dragon descending from the sky.

With Deoxys' barrier gone, Rayquaza flipped in the air and began flying downward.

Its golden eyes gleamed with a hint of surprise.

Didn't expect that human to actually pull it off.

Not bad.

Now, it was time to eat.

HUM—

The first to notice Rayquaza's movements were Deoxys and Darkrai.

They simultaneously turned their gazes to the sky.

"This isn't good…"

Darkrai muttered.

Deoxys: We are the intruders here.

"Deoxys hasn't fully recovered."

Steven glanced at the purple-core Deoxys and the green-core it held. His voice was low.

Darkrai suddenly shuddered.

It stiffly turned around—

And met the expectant gazes of Gengar, Ninetales, Steven, Fitzwilliam, and all the other Pokémon.

"You… You guys aren't thinking of making me fight Rayquaza, are you?"

That's not what we agreed on!

Deoxys clutched the green core and swept its gaze across Johan and the others.

Rayquaza was here because of it.

It had found its companion—there was no need to trouble them further.

It nodded to itself and, with a flash of its purple core, floated into the air.

Holding the green core to its chest, it shifted into Defense Forme, planning to force its way past Rayquaza's attack and escape into space.

Johan rubbed his forehead.

Nobody told you to move yet, why are you so eager…

And, sure enough—

Rayquaza had been in a relatively good mood, thinking it was finally about to eat.

But just as it was about to land, Deoxys shot into the sky.

In an instant—

Rayquaza realized it had been… tricked?

The "food" hadn't actually been subdued!

So it still had to take matters into its own hands?

Rayquaza's golden eyes flashed, and its body suddenly straightened.

A surge of energy coiled around it—

From afar, its body looked like a massive green arrow plummeting toward the ground.

Dragon Ascent!

Its speed was far beyond what Deoxys had anticipated.

"Raa?"

Dragonite tilted its head, watching Rayquaza, then looked down at its own hands, scratching its head.

It kinda looked similar… but also completely different.

BOOM!!

Deoxys' barrier shattered instantly.

Facing Rayquaza's full-power Dragon Ascent for the first time, it didn't even have time to dodge.

The speed it had used to ascend—

Was nothing compared to the speed at which it was now falling.

Like a meteor, it crashed into the ground.

The earth caved in.

A web of cracks spread from the impact site.

A massive cloud of dust billowed into the air.

"Garrr!!"

Rayquaza roared, looking down at Deoxys with an expression of disdain.

It opened its mouth, gathering energy at an alarming rate.

If this attack landed—

Even if Deoxys survived, at least half of Verdanturf Town would be wiped out!

Steven, Fitzwilliam, Drake, and Wallace all paled.

Rayquaza had suddenly gone all out, catching everyone off guard.

Well—

Not everyone.

Johan had predicted this.

He and Rayquaza had agreed to "prepare food," but while Deoxys was now calmed, they hadn't had a chance to explain the plan to it.

It had just charged in on its own.

Johan glanced at Gengar.

Gengar understood immediately and vanished into the shadows.

"I don't know if I can take this hit…"

Darkrai murmured.

"You don't need to."

Johan climbed onto Dragonite's back.

"But I'll need you to come with me."

He patted Dragonite's back.

"Raa~~"

Dragonite flapped its wings and activated Extreme Speed, shooting toward Rayquaza.

Darkrai hesitated for only a moment before following.

It didn't know what Johan was planning, but if it didn't go along—

Johan wouldn't last a single blow from Rayquaza.

"Rayquaza, stop!!"

Johan gripped the harness tightly and activated his Aura again.

Rayquaza, about to fire its attack at Deoxys, initially ignored him.

But then, out of the corner of its eye, it noticed Dragonite's aura during Extreme Speed.

It hesitated slightly.

The energy in its mouth slowly dissipated.

[You dare approach me?!]

Rayquaza's piercing gaze locked onto Johan.

Johan's heart pounded.

This pressure…

Even Dialga and Palkia hadn't been this intimidating.

Darkrai appeared beside him, easing some of the tension.

"That's Rayquaza…"

Darkrai whispered.

"We're gonna need a bigger bribe."

Johan smirked.

"Of course."

He gave Rayquaza a firm look.

"But I need a favor in return."

Rayquaza's golden eyes flickered as it sized up Johan and Dragonite.

The air was heavy with tension.

For a brief moment, it seemed like Rayquaza was considering its options.

Then—

[I only teach this once.]

Rayquaza's voice resonated directly in Johan's mind.

"That's enough."

Johan glanced at Dragonite, giving it a silent signal.

Understanding immediately, Dragonite moved closer to Rayquaza.

This opportunity was rare—too rare to waste.

Darkrai, hovering nearby, rested its chin in its hand, observing with interest.

It then turned to Gengar, who was grinning mischievously.

…Did I sell myself too short back then?

Wait, no—

Wasn't it forced to learn this from Gengar in the first place?

Ah, whatever. What's done is done.

At least it had met Johan first.

First come, first served.

With that thought, Darkrai glanced at Rayquaza, an oddly smug expression in its eyes.

[Prepare yourself.]

"Raa!!"

Dragonite nodded seriously, eyes burning with determination.

And then—

Johan took a deep breath, gripping tightly onto Rayquaza's horns as the massive dragon shot forward, tearing through the sky like a streak of emerald lightning.

Dragonite followed close behind.

Its eyes never left Rayquaza's movements—watching, memorizing, learning.

The sky roared as the two dragons soared, pushing the limits of speed.

Johan, clinging onto Rayquaza's head, could feel the sheer power surging through its body.

It was an experience unlike anything before.

And in the distance—

Darkrai, still watching, muttered to itself.

"…Should I have asked for more?"

# 465: Are You the ‘Man Who Gets Everything Reimbursed’? (BONUS)

"Garrr—"

Rayquaza snorted, glancing at Johan, who had collapsed onto the ground the moment they landed. Two puffs of white air shot from its nostrils.

Pathetic.

One ride, and he's already down.

Johan's vision spun. The world twisted around him. For a moment, he swore he saw Rayquaza, Dragonite, and Latias sitting together playing "Guess Who?" with Pokémon cards.

…Huh?

Why was Latias there?

This was, without a doubt, the most dizzying experience of Johan's life.

I was a fool, truly.

He had only thought about how rare the opportunity to ride Rayquaza was—he had forgotten his "fear of extreme speeds."

Someone who can't even handle going too fast had actually sat on top of Rayquaza's head to experience Dragon Ascent firsthand?

This was beyond reckless—it was outright insane.

He shook his head, trying to clear his mind.

That said… riding Rayquaza was exhilarating.

And unlike certain past events, this wasn't some desperate battle where he had to push himself to the limit.

Unlike in the Pokémon Special manga, where Norman had to sacrifice himself to control Rayquaza, Johan was simply experiencing the ride firsthand.

Glancing at Dragonite, who seemed deep in thought, Johan figured it must have learned something from Rayquaza's "lesson."

Meanwhile, Rayquaza, still unimpressed, turned its gaze toward the two Deoxys—the one with the purple core and the one with the green core.

Its golden eyes narrowed.

A clear warning.

This time, I'll let you go. But if you enter my territory again without permission—don't blame me.

[Do not forget your promise.]

"Garrr—!!"

Rayquaza let out a powerful roar.

Its tail flicked, and with a single burst, it shot into the sky.

The clouds above were torn apart, leaving behind a pathway straight to space.

"Rayquaza, huh…"

Fitzwilliam muttered in awe.

Did Johan-sensei really just 'convince' Rayquaza to leave?

Was this the legendary "Aura Master's" charisma?

He recalled a phone call from Flint months ago, where Flint had told him that Johan had knocked out Dialga and Palkia.

At the time, he hadn't believed it.

But now?

Maybe that feeling Flint had back then… was exactly what he was feeling now.

Without hesitation, Fitzwilliam took out his phone and called Flint in the middle of the night.

"Hey, Afro-head, listen up—Johan-sensei just took a joyride on Rayquaza."

Johan, having recovered somewhat, turned toward the two Deoxys.

HUM—

His phone vibrated.

The screen turned green, displaying two words.

[█████]: Thank you.

Oh?

So it could say something other than "Friend" now?

Johan waved dismissively.

Earth is dangerous—go back to Mars.

That's what he thought.

What he actually said was, "As long as you're okay, that's what matters."

If he could help, he would. If he couldn't, then trying to act tough wouldn't change anything.

Luckily, his connection with Rayquaza had allowed for communication.

If they hadn't been able to talk, the entire Hoenn region would have been in deep trouble.

The two Deoxys exchanged glances.

Their facial scanning structures flickered.

After a moment, they nodded to each other, as if reaching a silent agreement.

Then—

Their arms intertwined, merging together.

From the fusion, they produced a mysterious metallic object—a small fragment woven with orange and blue.

It dropped to the ground.

Though only half the size of a fist, the impact made a metallic clang and left a small crater.

Clearly, it was extremely heavy.

"What is this…?"

Steven was visibly surprised.

Even he had never seen such a metal before.

Johan was also momentarily stunned.

A metal from outer space?

Or perhaps… a metal formed from Deoxys itself?

The two Deoxys didn't explain.

They simply nodded at Johan—then soared toward space together.

Just before leaving, Johan's phone lit up once again.

[█████]: Thank you.

Johan stared at his phone, his expression growing strange.

What the hell has my phone been through?

"Cha?"

Tinkaton cautiously approached.

It reached out, grabbed the metal fragment—

And lit up with excitement.

It squeezed the metal tightly.

At first, the ultra-dense material dented under Tinkaton's grip.

Then—

The metal slowly repaired itself.

Tinkaton's eyes sparkled.

"Cha!!"

The final piece of the puzzle for Charcadet's armor—was complete!

With this, the armor would be perfect.

Was it finally time for Charcadet to evolve?

The situation seemed to be settling down.

"Johan. Steven."

Drake and Wallace finally approached.

They should have arrived earlier, but when Johan somehow calmed Rayquaza down, they had decided not to interfere.

The last thing they wanted was for Rayquaza or Deoxys to mistake them for a threat.

Now that both legendary Pokémon had left, they could finally step in.

Viridian City, Kanto—Rocket Team Secret Base.

"Wait, wait—it really evolved?!"

As the evolutionary light faded, the figure of Porygon2 was replaced by… something new.

Its red and blue colors remained, but its eyes had turned yellow, resembling a bullseye.

Its entire body had taken on a stranger shape, its movements even more unnatural.

More importantly—

Porygon2 had always retained some level of programmed control.

But now?

Even through the screen, the researchers could sense its intelligence.

It no longer felt like a machine.

It felt alive.

HUM—

Suddenly, the newly evolved Pokémon flickered—

—And appeared behind the researchers.

A scientist gasped.

"Wait… is that a dimensional ability?!"

It tilted its head, studying them.

Just like they had once studied it.

And its behavior was… odd.

Its head remained perfectly still, while its body rotated in circles.

The patterns on its body shifted, glowing in different colors—

One moment, gold with electric sparks.

The next, black with ominous energy.

Then, blue with an eerie glow.

It was as if its own type was shifting continuously.

But it didn't attack.

It simply… observed.

At that moment—

A figure appeared on the upper platform, watching with a subtle smile.

"Now this… is interesting."

"Boss Giovanni!"

The researchers immediately stood at attention.

The new Porygon turned.

It stared at Giovanni.

Then—

Dove back into the screen.

The researchers exhaled in relief.

But Giovanni's gaze remained fixed on something else.

Not the Porygon—

But Mewtwo.

His eyes darkened.

Even without proof, he already knew.

This had something to do with Johan.

Back in Verdanturf Town—Steven's Mansion.

"So you're saying all those rampaging Pokémon… were caused by the collaboration between Team Magma and Greater Mauville Holdings?"

Drake frowned as Johan explained.

It sounded like a question—

But from his tone, he already believed it.

The history between Devon Corporation and Greater Mauville Holdings was no secret to him.

Johan took a sip of coffee, then lowered his voice.

"Old man, I've got a question."

Drake glanced at him.

"Go ahead."

Johan tapped his fingers on the table.

"Has the League… ever attempted to capture Rayquaza?"

Drake froze.

His eyes widened.

"You… how do you know about that?"

Johan sat up straight.

"So it's true?!"

And just as Johan was processing this revelation—

A golden-haired woman appeared before him.

She smiled.

"Are you… the 'Man Who Gets Everything Reimbursed'?"

# 466: It’s Time to Burn Everything, Charcadet! (BONUS)

"The Man Who Gets Everything Reimbursed?"

Johan slowly focused on the figure before him. He blinked, then sat up from the ground with a surprised expression.

"Elite Four Glacia?"

Golden hair coiled into an elegant updo, a regal purple low-cut dress, pristine white gloves, and pale green eyes...

She was none other than Glacia, one of Hoenn's Elite Four and the overseer of the Hoenn League's finances—er, rather, the one in charge of reimbursements!

Why was she here?

Oh, right.

Last night, Rayquaza and Deoxys had clashed. As one of Hoenn's Elite Four, it was natural for her to be called in for emergencies.

Still, Johan hadn't expected to run into her here at Steven's mansion in Verdanturf City after everything was already settled.

Glacia covered her mouth with a refined chuckle. "It's a pleasure to meet you, Johan-sensei."

Johan stood up, feeling slightly awkward.

After all… he had just sent a reimbursement request to Drake.

But wait—wasn't Drake supposed to be the one signing off on it?

Johan didn't know much about Glacia. Even back when he played the games, there wasn't much information about her.

He did know that she specialized in Ice-types and that she wasn't originally from Hoenn.

She had moved to Hoenn because of its warmer climate.

Glacia had a unique approach to training Ice Pokémon—she believed that they didn't need to be in icy environments all the time.

In fact, training in warmer climates could help Ice Pokémon overcome their natural weaknesses.

It was similar to Cynthia's training methods—she often took her Garchomp to snowy mountains like Mt. Coronet to build resistance to Ice-type attacks.

As for why a non-Hoenn native became part of Hoenn's Elite Four...

That wasn't unusual in the Pokémon world.

For example, Drasna was originally from Sinnoh but became an Elite Four member in Kalos.

In the future, Koga, a Kanto Gym Leader, would later become an Elite Four member in Johto.

As long as someone could contribute, their region of origin didn't matter.

After Johan stood up, Glacia took out a piece of paper, her expression playful as she read aloud:

"500 Oran Berries, 500 Rawst Berries, 500 Aspear Berries, 500 Lum Berries..."

Johan's expression stiffened slightly.

That was the exact reimbursement list he had sent to Drake.

Sure, the quantity was a bit excessive—

But considering he had used up less than 100 berries' worth of Pokéblocks and still filed for reimbursement, he had already been pushing it.

Then—

Glacia pulled out another paper.

"5000 Oran Berries, 5000 Rawst Berries, 5000 Aspear Berries, 5000 Lum Berries..."

Huh?!

Johan's eyes widened in shock.

This was his exact list—but with everything multiplied by ten.

A moment later, he understood.

This was Drake's reimbursement request.

This was 'middleman profit' in action!

Johan had thought his reimbursement request was already outrageous—

But Drake's?

Even worse!

No wonder they call him 'Reimbursement King'!

Glacia smirked, putting away the papers.

"Bad habits start from the top. If the old man misbehaves, of course, the younger generation follows suit."

Her voice turned cold.

Johan sweatdropped.

Drake, old man… can you not mess around like this?!

"Johan-sensei," Glacia's tone softened again. She smiled. "Should I approve your reimbursement request?"

"Ahem."

Johan cleared his throat, noticing the way she looked at him.

After a moment of silence, he nodded seriously and said, "Yes. You should."

"Oh?" Glacia raised an eyebrow. "And why is that?"

Johan spread his hands. "This was all necessary expenses—many costs aren't immediately visible…"

"How about this?"

Before he could finish, Glacia interrupted him.

"We're all trainers here. I've heard of your strength, Johan-sensei. Let's have a battle. If you win, I'll approve your request."

…Wait.

Isn't this abuse of power?!

"Elite Four Glacia, you know what happened last night. My Pokémon's condition…"

Before Johan could finish, Glacia held up two fingers.

"Double."

"This isn't about how many times I—I always file reimbursements honestly—"

"Triple."

"Accepted!"

Whew—

Johan exhaled.

That many berries, tripled?

That was enough for Gengar alone to eat for two months.

If he rationed it properly, the whole team could eat for a month.

Especially since some of those berries were rare varieties.

So… now he understood what she meant by "bad habits start from the top."

Glacia was Drake's "younger generation" in terms of seniority.

No wonder she was just as bad!

Should've asked Darkrai to stick around longer…

"Don't worry," Glacia said with a smile. "I know what you all went through last night. This is just a friendly match—just for fun."

Then, she added, "Besides, since I'm being so 'reasonable,' I have a small favor to ask in return."

Johan narrowed his eyes.

"What kind of favor?"

"It's about Team Magma and Team Aqua. There's a meeting, and I'd like you to attend."

A meeting related to Team Magma and Team Aqua?

That was worth going to.

If they could eliminate both teams early, maybe the Groudon vs. Kyogre disaster would never happen.

That would make Hoenn a much safer place.

After a brief pause, Johan nodded.

"Fine."

It was just a meeting, after all.

Not some dangerous mission.

Meanwhile—

"Drake, you're really going all out, huh?"

A young man—wearing a *black short-sleeved shirt, dark red capri pants, and loafers—*grinned as he leaned back lazily. His mohawk-styled hair added to his delinquent vibe.

He was Hoenn's Dark-type Elite Four member, Grimsley.

Drake sighed heavily.

Then, straightened his expression and said,

"This was the only way."

Of course, he wouldn't admit that he had been caught red-handed by Glacia while trying to "adjust" the reimbursement form.

Instead, he added, "Johan has the potential to be more than just a teacher. He needs the right motivation to push himself."

Grimsley's expression was unreadable.

He saw right through Drake.

But hey—

Drake and Glacia's 'strategy' was working.

Even though they didn't realize that Johan didn't actually need any bribery to take down Team Magma and Team Aqua.

If Groudon and Kyogre really woke up—

Hoenn might be destroyed.

And if that happened—

Where was Johan supposed to go to be a teacher?

Drake, however, had his own long-term plan.

If Johan kept attending League meetings, then one day…

Even if Johan refused to become an Elite Four member, Drake could force him to handle things as his "representative."

Drake grinned inwardly.

Good thing he recruited Johan early.

At Steven's mansion—

"Elite Four Glacia versus Johan?"

Steven and Wallace exchanged glances.

It wasn't surprising for trainers to battle on a whim.

And Johan had fought Elite Four members before.

Still—

"Who do you think will win?" Wallace asked.

Steven thought for a moment.

"It depends on how much they both hold back."

"And if they both go all out?"

"Johan."

Without hesitation.

Wallace nodded thoughtfully.

Meanwhile—

Johan looked at Charcadet.

"Charcadet."

"Goka?"

The little Pokémon looked up.

Johan smiled warmly.

"It's time to burn everything."

"Goka!!"

# 467: Burn, Ceruledge! (BONUS)

Steven's Battle Arena.

Glacia and Johan stood on opposite sides of the field.

Since this was just a simple battle, there was no need for a referee.

The elegant and noble Glacia glanced at Johan and tossed her Poké Ball with a smile.

"Go, Vanilluxe!"

The Pokémon that appeared resembled a giant ice cream cone, covered in snow, with two heads resting atop an icy base.

"Vanilluxe, the Blizzard Pokémon. Ice-type. It is said to form when two Vanillish merge together—Roto~ slurp—"

Slurp?

Johan turned to his Rotom Pokédex with suspicion. That detail was definitely something it added on its own.

To be fair, even Gengar and the others had similar reactions.

"Ka-kaah~!"

Larvitar couldn't help but swallow, as if imagining something delicious.

Ice cream!

Honestly, this little one was kind of adorable—maybe it would even taste good?

Like Alcremie, Vanilluxe was a favorite among many pastry chefs.

The difference was that Alcremie produced fresh cream, while Vanilluxe made shaved ice.

Gengar and the others' eyes twinkled as they looked at Johan.

Should we get one too?

Wouldn't that mean unlimited ice cream?!

Unlimited ice cream?

Were they underestimating Ninetales?

Johan rolled his eyes but spoke calmly.

"Charcadet."

"Goka!"

Charcadet, already prepared, stepped forward with flickering flames, taking steady steps into the arena.

"Garruu!!"

The Urshifu in the group cheered loudly for Charcadet.

"Charcadet, huh?" Glacia's smile didn't waver.

Fire-types did have the advantage over Ice-types.

But the reason she had brought her Pokémon to live in Hoenn was to train them in adapting to the heat, reducing Fire's effectiveness against Ice.

Hoo—

And sure enough—

The moment Charcadet stepped onto the battlefield, snowflakes began to fall. Within seconds, the ground was covered with a thin layer of snow.

Snow Warning!

Just like Ninetales, Vanilluxe's ability was Snow Warning.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales, lounging nearby, half-opened its eyes. This Vanilluxe's Snow Warning was not bad.

White snowflakes and crimson flames contrasted sharply in the battlefield. The moment the snow touched Charcadet, it instantly melted into droplets, then evaporated into steam.

With each step Charcadet took, it left behind deep blackened footprints, carving a snow-free path across the field.

As Charcadet stood firm, a small vacuum seemed to form around it, repelling the snow.

Glacia's smile deepened. "Impressive flames."

But—

No matter how strong the fire, it couldn't completely overcome the environment.

The flames around Charcadet dimmed slightly.

Johan looked at Charcadet and said softly, "Go all out. If we lose, it's on me."

"Goka!"

Charcadet took its stance. Its flames flared wildly, its crimson and ghostly blue eyes burning with the thirst for victory.

Johan had told it to fight freely, to show its full power without worrying about winning or losing.

But—

I want to get stronger!

I want to help Johan win every battle!!!

"Goka!!"

Charcadet's flames roared, as if its Blaze ability had been activated.

No matter how cold the snow and ice were, the fire within burned hotter than ever!

With a determined cry, Charcadet charged at Vanilluxe at high speed.

Flare Blitz!

"What a… fierce presence." Glacia murmured in slight disbelief.

This was the fighting spirit between Fire-type trainers and their Pokémon?

A worthy opponent.

Then—

To honor this opponent, she had to go all out as well!

"Vanilluxe, Blizzard!"

A fierce icy wind howled, whipping up a storm of snow and ice shards. The already freezing battlefield dropped even further in temperature as a raging snow tornado engulfed the field.

Amid the endless white, a streak of crimson fire cut through the storm, charging forward with unwavering resolve!

A trainer's duty—

Was to believe in their Pokémon.

That was enough!

Johan clenched his fist, pulling up his collar slightly against the cold.

No more words were needed.

Go, Charcadet!

BOOM!!

Flames exploded outward, colliding fiercely with the snowstorm. Red and white spiraled into a rising vortex.

A powerful shockwave burst from the point of impact, scattering fire and snow in all directions.

Vanilluxe remained floating in place, though a large portion of its body showed signs of melting.

But—

Its melted parts rapidly regenerated before their eyes.

Meanwhile, Charcadet was thrown back, carving two deep, charred trenches into the ground. The flames on its body dimmed, and frost clung to its arms, shoulders, and knees.

"Amazing spirit, incredible determination."

Wallace sighed in admiration.

Steven furrowed his brows slightly. "But the gap in strength between Charcadet and Vanilluxe isn't something that can be overcome with spirit alone."

No matter how much willpower one had, there were limits.

"Goka!"

Charcadet let out an unwilling growl, and once again, flames erupted from within, melting the ice on its body.

Though it still looked powerful, Wallace and Steven could see it—

Charcadet's fire was slowing down.

But Johan and Charcadet's resolve had already spread to Glacia and Vanilluxe.

They wouldn't hold back.

This was their chance!

"Vanilluxe, Blizzard again!"

The snowstorm intensified, and as the battle dragged on, Glacia's strategy became clearer.

The longer the fight continued, the stronger her Ice-type attacks would become!

A true Ice-type specialist!

The field turned into an ice-covered world. The freezing wind made even those watching shiver.

At the heart of the blizzard, Charcadet endured the cold, slowly lowering its stance as its flames withdrew inward.

Within its chest, a volcano rumbled, waiting to erupt.

"Charcadet!"

Johan stood at the edge of the battlefield, feeling the same freezing winds but never retreating.

"Overheat!!"

For a moment, everything fell eerily silent.

Then—

BOOM!!

A volcano of fire erupted into the sky, consuming the battlefield in a blazing inferno.

The blizzard and snow instantly turned into thick steam, swirling upward.

"This is… a warrior's determination."

Glacia murmured.

But then, she clenched her fist.

She had seen this spirit before—

"Vanilluxe, Double Blizzard!"

Both of Vanilluxe's heads roared, unleashing an even stronger snowstorm!

The entire battlefield, trees, and even nearby buildings became covered in thick layers of ice.

This was insane!

As the winds settled, an ice sculpture stood at the center of the battlefield.

Deep inside, frozen solid, was Charcadet—

Motionless.

"Ka-kaah!"

"Garruu!"

Johan's Pokémon cried out, nearly rushing into the battlefield.

Vanilluxe panted—defeating those flames had not been easy.

Glacia showed no joy at her apparent victory. Instead, she nodded respectfully.

"Johan… your flames—"

But Johan interrupted her.

He smiled.

"…We haven't lost yet."

Huh?

CRACK!

A fracture split across the massive ice sculpture.

At its center, a small golden ember flickered—

Then blazed to life.

BOOM!!

The ice shattered.

Charcadet kneeled on one knee, breathing heavily, steam rising from its mouth.

Its flames flickered weakly—

But its eyes burned with even greater determination!

Glacia was momentarily stunned. Then, a smile spread across her face.

What a battle.

Johan tossed something toward Charcadet—

A piece of armor, crafted by Tinkaton.

Charcadet caught it and effortlessly donned the armor.

"Burn bright, Ceruledge!"

Henshin!

A radiant light engulfed Charcadet.

Its silhouette stretched taller, and ghostly blue flames replaced the white glow.

With a swift motion, it unsheathed a blade of spectral fire—

Ceruledge had emerged!

"Evolution?!" Wallace's eyes widened.

Steven smirked.

"That's… cool."

Johan grinned as he admired Ceruledge's new form.

"Being cool is a lifelong pursuit."

Ceruledge swung its flaming sword, its eyes and body burning even brighter with the power of evolution.

But the battle wasn't over yet!

"Vanilluxe!"

Glacia quickly regained focus. Snow and ice gathered once more, sweeping across the battlefield.

A glowing orb shot out from Vanilluxe's body—

Weather Ball!

During a snowstorm, Weather Ball turned into an Ice-type move, and its power doubled!

Unlike Blizzard's wide-range attack, Weather Ball was more focused—making it even more lethal.

BOOM!

The icy sphere crashed directly into Ceruledge—only for its body to dissolve into mist.

Glacia's eyes widened.

Phantom Force?

Gengar grinned from the sidelines. Even though Ceruledge's evolution might challenge its title as the team's coolest Pokémon, this particular move?

That was all thanks to Gengar's own training.

WHOOSH—

Suddenly, a dark figure appeared behind Vanilluxe.

The ghostly blue glow in Ceruledge's eyes flickered with cunning intent.

"Bitter Blade!"

Johan's voice rang out as Ceruledge's sword trembled with eerie flames.

A single slash—

A deep, burning wound.

Vanilluxe staggered backward, its body trembling—

And as if that wasn't enough, the ghostly flames from Bitter Blade clung to its body, eating away at it.

So fast!

After evolving, Ceruledge's speed had skyrocketed!

Not only that—Bitter Blade healed it.

And in the midst of all this, that golden ember in its chest flared even brighter.

In an instant—

Ceruledge's stamina was fully restored!

"Again! Bitter Blade!" Johan commanded.

"Goka!!"

Ceruledge let out a fierce cry, its body bending slightly like a drawn bow, then—

WHOOSH!

It shot forward at lightning speed!

"Vanilluxe, Blizzard! Protect yourself!" Glacia called.

Another snowstorm erupted, wrapping around Vanilluxe like a protective barrier.

The freezing winds howled.

Even though the snowflakes didn't physically attack, the cold alone was enough to weaken an opponent.

Even evolved, Ceruledge couldn't ignore this brutal, numbing temperature.

Ice crystals formed on its armor, causing fine cracks to appear.

But—

Rather than slow down, it became faster.

Weak Armor!

Charcadet's evolution had replaced Flame Body with Weak Armor—

And even ice damage triggered its effect.

The colder the battlefield got—

The faster Ceruledge became!

Charging through the storm, the flaming sword slashed through the blizzard, growing larger in Vanilluxe's vision.

Its ghostly blue glow flickered deep within Vanilluxe's icy heart—

BOOM!!

The blade struck cleanly.

For a moment, there was only silence.

Then—

The blizzard itself split apart, as if cut in two by the sword.

A massive gap opened in the swirling ice tornado, its edges unnaturally smooth and precise.

It was like the entire storm had been sliced in half.

Vanilluxe let out a pained cry as it was knocked out of the air, crashing heavily onto the battlefield.

A deep silence settled over the arena.

The snow stopped.

The wind died down.

And then—

Vanilluxe fainted.

Glacia took a deep breath.

"…I lost."

"Goka!"

Ceruledge let out a slow breath, standing proudly before Johan.

They did it.

Johan walked forward, patting its shoulder with a satisfied smile.

"Good job."

Ceruledge barely had time to nod before—

CRACK—

A new fracture appeared in its armor.

Ceruledge: Σ(дlll)

Tinkaton: Σ(дlll)

Johan: "…"

"Uh. Kinda fragile, huh?"

No problem. Tinkaton could patch it up.

But now that Johan thought about it—

According to Tinkaton, this armor was reversible.

If Ceruledge flipped it around—

Would it become Armarouge?

And if—

Only half of it was flipped?

Would that make it…

Omnimon?!

# 468: This Makes No Sense! (BONUS)

"Ka-kaah!"

At Gengar's excited cry, the others immediately swarmed forward.

They ruffled Ceruledge's helmet, rubbed its armor, and even touched its sword—

And to be honest, Tinkaton's craftsmanship was pretty impressive.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar patted Ceruledge's shoulder with satisfaction.

Not bad. From now on, your looks will only be second to mine.

Even Ceruledge, who usually looked so cool and composed, couldn't help but seem a little shy in the face of all the attention.

At the same time, it had gained a new understanding of itself.

In terms of appearance, it was definitely one of the best-looking in the team.

"That was an amazing battle."

Glacia had finished tending to Vanilluxe and approached with a light clap.

"Charcadet's battle helped it experience change and growth. Strengthening its beliefs, then evolving at the perfect moment to solidify that conviction and secure victory."

As a member of the Elite Four, Glacia had raised many Pokémon. She could clearly see Johan's approach to training Charcadet.

No wonder he was called the 'Master of the Mind'—he had an incredible grasp of Pokémon psychology.

"It worked hard on its own. I just helped it recognize its own strength," Johan replied with a smile.

Glacia nodded.

Self-awareness was a strength in itself.

"You won. I'll approve your reimbursement request."

She accepted her loss without hesitation.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar glanced at the others smugly—another victory, another payday.

Then, Glacia stepped closer to Johan and lowered her voice.

"Interested in taking my job?"

Huh?!

Johan's eyes widened.

"You'd be able to approve your own reimbursements." Glacia continued, tempting him.

Wait, hold on—

Johan nearly choked.

First Drake, and now even Glacia?

What was going on with the Hoenn Elite Four?!

Of course, Glacia wasn't asking Johan to replace her as an Elite Four member.

What she meant was—once Johan inevitably became one, he should also take over her financial management responsibilities.

Because being an Elite Four in Hoenn was… exhausting.

Just look at the team:

Drake, the strongest among them, spent most of his time sailing the seas in pursuit of some grand conquest.

Sidney, the Dark-type specialist, used to be a small-time gangster. Sure, he'd turned over a new leaf, and he had a strong sense of justice… but when it came to management?

Forget it.

Then there was Phoebe, the recently appointed Ghost-type Elite Four. Her strength and potential were undeniable, but she had spent most of her life on Mt. Pyre with her grandparents.

She had zero experience in administration.

Which left…

Glacia.

And she was completely overwhelmed with work.

After hearing her explanation, Johan finally understood.

This was basically Hoenn's version of Lucian's situation in Sinnoh.

Managing Hoenn's finances sounded tempting—there was so much money involved.

As a famous leader once said, money was just a string of numbers.

But in the end, it wasn't his money.

And—

The more power you had, the heavier your responsibilities became.

Johan had no grand ambitions.

Right now, he just wanted to take care of his own team first.

Then, if he had the energy, maybe he'd think about everyone else.

His own team was already expensive enough—he didn't have time to manage Hoenn's budget.

"Maybe next time," Johan said with an awkward smile. "Or… maybe not."

"I'm going to check on Ceruledge."

With that, he quickly escaped into the house.

Huh?

Maybe not?

Shouldn't he have said, 'Next time for sure'?!

Glacia blinked.

Then, she smiled.

That was fine.

Because in the end—

Johan would have no choice but to take responsibility.

The more he got involved, the harder it would be to escape.

That was just how things worked.

Feeling satisfied, Glacia turned to Steven and Wallace, giving them a small nod.

Right.

These two could also be useful in the future.

After all, they were Hoenn's future leaders.

"Master Johan really won," Steven murmured after witnessing the entire battle.

He didn't seem surprised by the result.

To be honest, neither he nor Wallace would've been surprised regardless of who won.

It was just a matter of how much strength each of them revealed.

The only unexpected part—

Was that Johan used this battle to help Charcadet evolve.

Wallace was still replaying the fight in his mind, his eyes shimmering with interest.

"That sword strike…"

It was both fast and powerful.

It had a graceful elegance—

Similar to the ice sculptures created by Ninetales.

Wait—

Did Master Johan have a deep understanding of Contest Battles as well?

Back inside, Johan finally had a chance to examine Ceruledge properly.

In his mind, his Pokédex data appeared—

[Ceruledge]

Type: Fire/Ghost

Ability: Weak Armor

Moveset: Mean Look, Leer, Ember, Astonish, Clear Smog, Fire Spin, Will-O-Wisp, Night Shade, Flame Charge, Incinerate, Smog, Flamethrower, Overheat, Bitter Blade

Mood: Determined, Confident

("Defeating others is easy—defeating myself is the real challenge! The only opponent I must overcome… is me!")

Condition: Excellent

(Its armor seems very different from the usual 'Cursed Armor.' There are many hidden mechanisms within it.)

Friendship: 100

("I will make Johan the greatest trainer in the world!")

Ceruledge's stamina was a little low after the battle, but a quick rest would restore it.

At first glance, it didn't seem too different from an ordinary Ceruledge.

But—

It already knew Bitter Blade and Shadow Claw.

Soon, it would probably learn Swords Dance from Tinkaton.

With its speed and Bitter Blade's healing ability, Ceruledge would be a nightmare for opponents.

Especially since it had received Entei's blessing and carried a small fragment of the Sacred Fire of Life—

Bitter Blade's recovery effects were already showing clear results.

Johan could already see it—

'Hah! You can't even damage me—I heal more than you can hit!'

A life-stealing swordsman—who wouldn't fear that?

Then, he noticed something unusual in the Condition entry.

There was a special note about Ceruledge's armor.

Johan had assumed Tinkaton would forge two separate suits—one for each form.

But instead—

She only made one.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton smirked proudly.

This was her masterpiece.

She motioned for Ceruledge to do something.

"Goka?"

Following her instructions, Ceruledge grabbed a small lever on its chest—

And twisted it 180 degrees.

WHOOSH—

A strange glow enveloped its armor.

Gengar immediately recognized this energy—

It was the power of Celebi's time manipulation!

At the same time, the armor shifted and reformed, similar to Deoxys' ability to change forms.

Johan's eyes widened.

Wait—so that's it?!

No wonder Tinkaton only forged one suit.

This armor—

It could transform!

Flames erupted from Ceruledge's body—

And when the fire faded…

It was no longer Ceruledge.

It had become—

Armarouge.

Armarouge emerged from the fading flames, its golden armor gleaming like that of a proud warrior. A knight's helmet adorned its head, its crest resembling flames. Beneath the armor, its body was no longer ghostly blue but a deep, fiery crimson—like Charcadet had matured into a true warrior.

Armarouge!

"Armarouge, the Fire Warrior Pokémon. Fire/Psychic-type. It specializes in honorable duels and will defeat even the strongest of foes in direct combat—Roto! So cool!"

Rotom's voice trembled with excitement, as if it had been momentarily stunned by Armarouge's sheer presence.

It really worked!

Even though Johan had mentally prepared himself, he was still surprised that Tinkaton had actually succeeded in forging an armor that allowed for this transformation.

Sure, they had rare materials, and they had help from Entei, Celebi, and Deoxys, among others—

But still…

This was one of a kind in the entire Pokémon world!

Johan glanced at Tinkaton and muttered in amazement, "Tinkaton… you're practically a forging master now."

Armarouge, having transformed, inspected its hands and ran them over its armor, as if confirming its new state.

It could clearly feel the difference in power compared to before.

Meanwhile, Johan's Pokédex data updated itself.

[Armarouge]

Type: Fire/Psychic

Ability: Weak Armor

Moveset: Mean Look, Leer, Ember, Astonish, Clear Smog, Fire Spin, Will-O-Wisp, Night Shade, Flame Charge, Incinerate, Smog, Flamethrower, Overheat, Armor Cannon, Psyshock…

There it was—

Armor Cannon!

Armarouge's signature move. A 120-power Fire-type attack, stronger than Overheat, with perfect accuracy.

The downside? It lowered both Defense and Special Defense after use.

But who cared?!

With this level of destructive power, it was absolutely worth it!

"Too bad… Bitter Blade isn't available anymore." Johan sighed.

Still—

This was already beyond incredible.

He wouldn't dare ask for more.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton suddenly raised a finger and wagged it at Johan, as if saying: 'Not so fast.'

Huh?

She hopped over to Armarouge, gesturing excitedly.

Armarouge hesitated for a moment, then reached for its chest again—

And twisted the armor back.

WHOOSH—

A surge of ghostly blue flames erupted once more—

And in an instant, Ceruledge had returned.

What. The. Hell.

Rotom, Gengar, and Larvitar all reacted the same way—

Shock.

Their eyes sparkled with awe, much like a child seeing a superhero transformation for the first time.

Johan was just as stunned.

Tinkaton had actually done it.

"Wait a second… this means…"

Tinkaton smirked proudly.

Ceruledge could freely switch between Armarouge and Ceruledge using its armor.

Johan almost wanted to play theme music—

Something dramatic, something that screamed, 'This is peak awesomeness!'

Maybe something like…

'Big Brain Energy'…

No, wait. That was a meme.

Regardless—

This was insane!

Johan quickly noticed something, though.

While Ceruledge seemed like its natural state, transforming into Armarouge required extra energy.

Every transformation took a toll on its stamina.

"Cha~~"

Tinkaton gestured again.

This time, Ceruledge moved differently.

It twitched its left hand, and suddenly—

The armor split open.

Johan's eyes widened—

No way.

The ghostly blue sword was replaced by a golden cannon.

WHAT?!

THIS REALLY WAS OMEGAMON!

Johan almost spat out his drink.

He suddenly recalled Tinkaton's original blueprints for Charcadet's armor, where it had both a sword and a cannon.

At the time, they thought it was too ridiculous.

But now—

Here it was.

One arm held a ghostly blue blade.

The other arm had a fiery golden cannon.

For a brief moment—

The Pokédex glitched.

It actually couldn't recognize what this form was.

Then—

POOF!

The cannon vanished.

Ceruledge immediately stumbled, dropping to one knee.

The energy drain was too much.

Johan's mind raced.

Was the burden too great?

Or…

Was it just a matter of getting used to it?

For a split second, the Pokédex had recognized the change—

But the form had disappeared too quickly to register.

"Cha?"

Tinkaton tilted her head, deep in thought, tapping her hammer against her palm.

Clearly, she was already theorizing ways to improve it.

Johan suddenly had a wild thought.

What if…

During this transformation, he infused Ceruledge with Aura?

After all—Armarouge could use Aura Sphere.

Would that help?

No, no—too risky for now.

The last thing he needed was someone trying to capture Ceruledge for 'research.'

The Pokémon world was already full of crazy things.

Team Rocket had once developed an Eevee that could change evolutions at will without needing stones.

And let's not forget—

There was a fusion experiment combining all three Legendary Birds into one monster.

Compared to that, Ceruledge's transformation almost seemed normal.

Johan sighed, giving Ceruledge some Pokéblocks to help it recover.

Meanwhile, Tinkaton got to work patching up the armor, hammering away at any cracks.

Johan's eye twitched.

He finally noticed something.

The materials she was using…

Were all extremely rare metals.

How much was this costing him?!

Was every battle from now on going to be burning money?!

Of course, Ceruledge could repair itself over time.

But Tinkaton was a perfectionist. She refused to let even a single scratch remain.

Johan stared at the ceiling.

His expenses had just skyrocketed.

Once Tinkaton finished, Gengar, Rotom, and Larvitar immediately pulled her aside.

They had requests.

Ceruledge's armor was too cool.

Gengar wanted its own armor.

Rotom wanted to be a transforming robot.

And Larvitar…

Wanted everything.

Because, after all—

"Only kids make choices. I want it all!"

# 469: Johan’s Team Training Exercise

I've realized giving extra chapters does nothing in terms of boosting rankings so I'm thinking of stopping them.

-----------

"Good morning, Professor."

"Good morning, Professor Johan."

"Professor Johan~"

Returning to the academy, Johan entered the training room with a smile, greeting the students who called out to him.

The incident in Verdanturf Town the other night wasn't exactly a secret. After all, with so many residents—despite being evacuated to shelters—the commotion was too big to ignore.

Not to mention, the legendary 'Sky Dragon' Rayquaza had appeared!

"I heard Professor Johan fought Rayquaza?"

"What do you mean 'fought'? He beat Rayquaza!"

"I heard he even caught it!"

"For real?"

"A bunch of people saw him riding on Rayquaza's head! If he didn't catch it, how could he ride it?"

"Whoa! Riding Rayquaza?"

"No wonder they call him 'Legend Slayer'!"

Someone who only fights Legendary Pokémon!

Of course, all of this was just rumors.

Even the story of Johan riding Rayquaza's head—while not entirely false—was still exaggerated.

After all, with Deoxys disrupting the town's electromagnetic fields, no one had been able to record anything with their devices.

And since there was no footage… the rumors ran wild.

Some people even brought up how Johan had previously smashed Dialga and Palkia, now claiming that he was on a mission to capture all seven Dragon-type Legendaries.

Johan just laughed it off.

There was no point in arguing.

Rumors spread fast, but denying them was pointless.

At this rate, he wouldn't be surprised if people started saying he had beaten Arceus next.

Most regular people and trainers just treated these as fun stories over tea.

But those who actually knew what happened?

They weren't fooled.

"Professor Johan! I heard you had Rayquaza pinned under you?!"

Hearing the shouting from the phone, Johan instinctively pulled it away from his ear.

Coming from Flint, the way he said it just sounded wrong.

"Yeah, yeah, next time I'll bring Rayquaza over to your house for a visit," Johan said dryly.

"My house?!" Flint gasped.

Would it still be standing after that?!

"H-haha… Professor Johan, you sure know how to joke—uh, anyway, Volkner's calling me for a battle. Gotta go! Bye!"

Click.

Johan smirked.

Flint, scared?

He'd have to tell Cynthia to beat him up some more next time.

Johan entered the training room.

He activated the protective barriers and summoned his Pokémon.

Gengar, Ninetales, Tinkaton, Dragonite, Urshifu, Ceruledge, and Larvitar.

Excluding Larvitar, his entire team had finally reached their final evolutions.

Crunch, crunch—

The moment Larvitar appeared, it immediately grabbed a mineral rock and started munching on it like a bag of chips.

Johan twitched.

He had underestimated Larvitar's appetite.

At this rate, the ore he mined two days ago would be completely devoured in a week.

Part of the problem was that Tinkaton had taken some of the metal-rich rocks for her hammer and to repair Ceruledge's armor.

So overall, he had mined a lot, but not enough to keep up with Larvitar's eating.

"Larvitar, eat some Pokéblocks too. You need a balanced diet."

"Yoji!"

Happily, Larvitar accepted the Pokéblocks.

Picky eater?

Not at all.

If Johan wasn't watching, Larvitar would've torn down the entire room and dug three feet into the ground by now.

Luckily, Glacia had approved his reimbursement request quickly.

Just this morning, several large crates of berries had arrived.

That should keep his Pokémon happy for a while.

"Ceruledge."

While the others trained on their own, Johan called over Ceruledge to assess its new abilities in detail.

Rotom hovered nearby, activating its recording mode for analysis.

"Goka~~"

Ceruledge held its dual swords at its sides, jogging over. Its blue-violet armor glowed faintly in the light.

It looked incredibly cool.

Even as its trainer, Johan couldn't help but admire its design every time he saw it.

"Let's spar a bit," Johan said.

Although Ceruledge had already used Bitter Blade and Shadow Claw in battle against Vanilluxe, its proficiency still needed improvement.

Today, they'd test its overall abilities.

"Goka!"

Ceruledge's sharp eyes gleamed as it nodded, blades flashing.

Johan grabbed a remote control, activating the training machines in the room.

"Get ready."

With a press of a button—

BANG! BANG-BANG—

Three spheres shot out at different speeds and angles across the room.

Ceruledge vanished in a blur, dashing toward the farthest sphere first.

Its ghostly blue flames flickered, slicing cleanly through the target—

And instantly incinerating it.

In a matter of moments, all three spheres were cut down.

Ceruledge returned to Johan's side without breaking a sweat.

Johan nodded in approval.

Its speed and precision were excellent—a sign that Charcadet's foundation had been trained properly.

"Time to increase the difficulty," Johan said.

He needed to find Ceruledge's limits before designing its next training plan.

"Goka!"

More spheres launched—each one faster than before.

Ceruledge cut them all down with expert precision.

But Johan kept increasing the difficulty, testing its reaction time, accuracy, and endurance.

With each challenge, he gained a clearer picture of Ceruledge's capabilities.

"Ceruledge's flames focus on precision and blade formation. In contrast, Armarouge…"

Johan stopped the exercise.

After letting Ceruledge catch its breath, he gestured for it to switch forms.

A twist of the armor's mechanism—

And with a burst of flames, Armarouge stood in Ceruledge's place.

Compared to Ceruledge, Armarouge was more suited for Psychic terrain control and large-scale attacks.

But for now, it hadn't learned moves like Expanding Force yet.

That could be trained later—right now, Johan wanted to test its firepower.

A heavy-duty training target emerged from the far side of the room.

"Armarouge."

Johan nodded toward it. "Try Armor Cannon—without any external power boosts."

That meant no Rainbow Feather, no Sacred Fire, no Aura, no Psychic enhancements—just raw Fire-type energy.

Armarouge nodded.

With a clicking sound, its golden arm plates shifted, forming a cannon over its arms.

WHOOSH—

A surge of intense heat radiated outward.

The air distorted around Armarouge as it gathered fire energy, drawing it into a single point.

For a moment—

The room darkened as the heat sucked in all available light.

Then—

BOOM!

A massive fire pillar shot forward, scorching a deep blackened trail across the floor.

The moment it struck the target—

KABOOM!!

The entire training room shook from the impact.

When the flames faded—

The target was gone.

Not damaged.

Not cracked.

Just completely vaporized.

Johan blinked.

"Yeah… that's way stronger than Charcadet's Overheat was."

And this was just the basic version.

If he added Sacred Fire, Aura, and Psychic power…

Johan shook his head.

Maybe it was better not to test that in here.

He didn't want to blow up the entire training facility.

For now, this was enough.

Johan carefully observed Armarouge as the last traces of heat dissipated from its cannon. The golden plating gleamed, cooling down after unleashing such concentrated firepower. A thin wisp of white smoke curled from the barrel, marking the residual energy of Armor Cannon.

That… had been ridiculously strong.

Stronger than any Overheat Charcadet had ever used before.

And this was without any additional boosts.

Johan rubbed his chin, deep in thought.

If Armarouge had this level of destructive power, then…

Would Ceruledge be able to match it in sheer offensive capability?

The answer wasn't so simple.

Armarouge specialized in powerful, concentrated ranged attacks, while Ceruledge focused on high-speed, sustained melee combat with Bitter Blade and its lifesteal effect.

Both forms had their strengths.

"Alright, next test—Aura Sphere."

Johan pressed another button on the control panel.

A medium-sized target emerged from the floor, reinforced for Special Attacks.

"Goka!"

Armarouge's body pulsed with Aura energy, and a deep blue glow gathered at the cannon's core.

Johan could feel it—his own Aura resonating slightly in response.

From the side, Urshifu glanced over, sensing the disturbance.

BOOM!

The Aura Sphere shot forward, its impact cleanly shattering the target.

The force was a little weaker than Armor Cannon, but the speed and precision made it an excellent spammable attack.

Johan nodded.

"Alright, that's enough for today."

With the tests completed, Armarouge twisted its chest piece once more—

And returned to Ceruledge.

Johan had considered testing that brief fusion form—where Ceruledge briefly gained a golden arm cannon—

But not yet.

It wasn't stable.

Not to mention, Ceruledge had only just evolved.

Mastering both forms was already a huge task.

There was no need to rush things.

Johan stretched and glanced at his team.

They had all been training independently during the session.

But when he turned toward Gengar—

The ghost was already clapping its hands together.

"Ka-kaah~~!"

With a mischievous grin, it beckoned the others.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

"Team battle?"

Gengar nodded.

Not the first time they'd done this.

When there weren't suitable opponents, the best solution was—

Fight each other.

Of course, this was just training—no one would go all out.

No Mega Evolution, no Aura, no special transformations like Reverse Scale.

Just pure skill and tactics.

"Alright," Johan agreed.

"Let's keep it friendly."

Rotom zipped forward excitedly.

"I'll handle the refereeing—Roto!"

Johan chuckled and stepped back, watching as his team prepared for battle.

Team Rankings (Unofficial):

Gengar – As Johan's first Pokémon, Gengar had the most experience and was the best at coordinating with him in battle.

Ninetales – Its defensive capabilities were almost untouchable. Even Gengar had a hard time breaking through its ice barriers.

Tinkaton – While slightly lacking in battle focus, its type advantage against Urshifu and Dragonite kept it in the top three.

Dragonite – Despite its power, Dragonite's ranking suffered because it couldn't use Reverse Scale in friendly matches.

Urshifu – Strong, but vulnerable to Fairy and Flying-types.

Ceruledge – Newly evolved and still adjusting to its full potential.

Larvitar – …Busy eating.

For now, Larvitar was the team's designated "free win."

But someday, it would climb the ranks.

Johan nodded as the battle began, already analyzing the matchups and strategies unfolding before him.

The more they fought together, the stronger they'd become—

And soon enough, he'd be able to take on even the strongest of Elite Four members.

# 470: Restoring the Glory of Grass-types—It’s Our Duty!

Generally speaking, a Pokémon's strength determines its status within a trainer's team.

But in Johan's case, things worked a little differently.

Because he treated all his Pokémon equally, even the non-battle-focused ones—like Rotom, Indeedee, and Alcremie—never seemed any weaker than Gengar and the others.

In his team, it wasn't strength that determined status, but ability.

There was a big difference between strength and ability.

For example, when the "Outlaw Squad" went on missions, Gengar took charge because of its experience and cunning.

But when it came to crafting and forging, whatever Tinkaton said was final—she was the boss.

And if they needed to search for ores, there was no doubt—Larvitar was the authority.

In short, the role of "leader" rotated, depending on the situation.

As Johan walked home, he thought about his Pokémon's future training plans.

Gengar, Ninetales, Tinkaton, and Dragonite were all steadily improving and didn't need any adjustments yet.

But the focus was on the recently evolved Urshifu and Ceruledge—

And, of course, Larvitar, who was still growing.

Larvitar was still young and just needed to grow happily for now.

Urshifu and Ceruledge, however, had forms that could change, meaning they had limitless potential—

But also required twice the effort to master.

And, of course…

Twice the resources.

Urshifu's Fighting-type moves were solid, so there wasn't much to add there.

For Dark and Water coverage, it already had Wicked Blow and Surging Strikes.

Sucker Punch and Aqua Jet provided mobility.

But it still needed coverage for Flying-types—so adding Thunder Punch, Ice Punch, Rock Slide, or Stone Edge was necessary.

However, Urshifu's biggest weakness was Fairy-types.

In Single Strike Style, it had a 4x weakness to Fairy.

Even in Rapid Strike Style, it was still 2x weak to Fairy.

So Iron Head, Iron Defense, and Metal Claw were a must-learn.

Compared to Urshifu, though—

Ceruledge had even more gaps to fill.

When it was a Charcadet, it had a very limited move pool, mostly Fire-type moves.

That made its typing too one-dimensional.

The only silver lining was that both Ceruledge and Armarouge had the same weaknesses:

Ground, Rock, Ghost, Water, and Dark.

Teaching them Water-type moves was out of the question.

But Grass-type? That was possible.

Ceruledge could learn Solar Blade.

Armarouge could learn Energy Ball and Solar Beam.

That would help against Ground, Rock, and Water-types.

The problem was—

Solar Blade and Solar Beam weren't cheap moves.

And Johan didn't have any Grass-type Pokémon to help them learn naturally.

They'd have to figure it out from scratch—which wasn't easy.

As for Ghost and Dark-type weaknesses—

Ceruledge could learn Poltergeist, a move perfect for its fighting style.

And Armarouge could master Shadow Ball.

Gengar could teach them both. That would save a lot of money.

For Dark-type counters, Ceruledge could learn Fighting-type moves, while Armarouge had Aura Sphere.

And Armarouge also needed to learn Psychic Terrain and Expanding Force.

Johan sighed.

Even if some moves could be self-taught, the materials for training would still cost a fortune.

And Ceruledge would have to invest a lot of time and energy into mastering them all.

"But overall, Solar Blade and Solar Beam are the most important moves right now."

Johan needed to find a trainer who specialized in Grass-types.

Buying the move tutor discs was an option, but it would take too long.

And Ceruledge already had too much to train in.

The less time wasted, the better.

Johan searched his memory for any elite Grass-type trainers.

After thinking for a while, he realized—

"Wait… there's no Grass-type Elite Four or Champion in the Pokémon world?"

That was… kind of shocking.

Too bad he hadn't thought about this back when he met Celebi.

"That means…"

Johan patted Ceruledge's shoulder, sighing dramatically.

"Restoring the glory of Grass-types—it's our duty!"

Ceruledge: ??

Grass-type?

Me?!

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar, with a white towel wrapped around its head, floated over to the window.

It stuck its head outside—

And scared the delivery Staraptor so badly that it nearly fainted mid-air.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar grinned, greeting the Staraptor as it grabbed a large package from its back.

"Another delivery?"

Johan had just finished showering after training.

Drying his wet hair with a towel, he glanced at Gengar.

Gengar nodded, curiously examining the box in its hands.

"Goooo~~(TT)"

A sorrowful cry came from outside the window.

Johan paused, looking at Gengar—who still had a towel wrapped around its head.

"Wait… you went to get the package like that?"

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar scratched its backside.

What's the problem?

Johan sighed, removing the towel from its head.

"Gengar, if you keep this up, no one's going to want to deliver here anymore."

Huh?

Johan had already given up on fixing Gengar's antics.

Instead, he turned his attention to the package, labeled "Rotom Mart."

Inside—

A foldable, portable bicycle!

"Lo-Lo-Lo-Roto!! (﹏)"

Rotom immediately sensed the built-in electric motor inside.

From Pokédex, to cleaning bot, to BBQ machine, to cameraman—

Now it was also a transportation tool?!

Johan smirked, seeing Rotom's reaction.

"It's the newest model. It can transform."

"Roto?!"

"Ka-kaah?!"

"Yoji?!"

Transform?!

Ever since Ceruledge had revealed its ability to change forms, these three had been obsessed with transformation.

They constantly pestered Tinkaton to build them armor or machines.

And now—

Rotom could actually transform into a bike?!

Johan grinned.

"The all-terrain, all-weather, adaptive Rotom Bicycle—"

"It fulfills all your needs!"

Riding through the city streets, Johan enjoyed the cool evening breeze.

The Rotom Bike was surprisingly smooth and fast, making the ride effortless.

Gengar floated beside him, sometimes stopping to chat with shopkeepers or passersby.

Meanwhile, Larvitar sat in the front basket, kicking its tiny legs as it curiously observed the surroundings.

"Left turn ahead, Roto!"

Rotom's voice-guided navigation activated.

But Johan ignored it.

He pedaled forward without turning.

"Error—incorrect route. Estimated arrival time increased by ten minutes—"

"Rotom, your map needs an update."

"Huh? Roto?!"

Johan pointed left.

"If I turned there, I'd crash into a wall."

There was a newly constructed building blocking the way.

"WHAT?!"

Rotom's voice shot up an octave.

Devon Corporation built that fast?!

Johan wasn't upset, though.

He patted Rotom's screen.

"Let's just stick to the main road."

Riding Rayquaza?

Pfft—

Why ride a dragon… when you can ride your own bike?

Besides—

Now he could finally cruise across water without slipping.

With the Rotom Bike, he didn't need to rely on Dragonite for transportation anymore.

Now that was a good investment.

Johan pedaled forward at a leisurely pace, the city lights reflecting off the sleek frame of his Rotom Bike. The evening air was crisp, carrying the faint scent of blooming flowers from the gardens lining the streets.

He wasn't in a hurry.

This was just a test ride—to see how well Rotom's modifications had integrated with the bike.

And so far…

It was perfect.

Smooth acceleration, effortless handling, and the best part—it carried Larvitar, Gengar, and even Ninetales with zero strain.

If this had been a normal bike, it would've collapsed under Larvitar's weight alone.

"Roto! Approaching intersection—recalculating optimal route!"

Johan ignored it again.

Instead, he veered slightly left, following the familiar path toward a plaza in the distance.

But as he approached—

His ears picked up a rhythmic, high-pitched melody.

Huh?

Street performers?

Curious, he slowed down and glanced toward the plaza.

What he saw made him do a double take.

A group of elderly men and women stood in a circle, moving in sync with a Loudred and several Whismur, all swaying to the beat of a catchy tune.

Johan blinked.

Was this… an exercise routine?

Or more importantly—

Was this a Pokémon-led dance class?!

"Ka-kaah…"

Beside him, Gengar's eyes sparkled with interest.

The ghostly grin on its face stretched wide.

This—this was its calling.

Singing, dancing, commanding an entire crowd…

Johan immediately recognized the danger.

He reached out and turned Gengar's head away.

"Don't even think about it."

"Ka-kaah?"

"If you start singing, half of them are going to pass out."

Gengar pouted.

He wasn't wrong.

If Gengar used its voice, it wouldn't be a concert—it'd be a mass KO event.

Johan shook his head.

Still… this whole setup reminded him of something.

If the elderly enjoyed Pokémon performances like this…

Wouldn't Jigglypuff have an entire market in this town?

A natural sleep aid, straight from the source.

Huh.

That might actually be a brilliant business idea.

Johan filed it away for later and continued riding.

Tonight wasn't about work—he was just enjoying the ride.

After making a few stops at the department store, the food district, and the battle arena, he finally felt satisfied.

The Rotom Bike had passed every test.

It was worth every Pokédollar spent.

Finally, he wouldn't need to rely on Dragonite for long-distance travel anymore.

As he locked up the bike outside his house, his phone buzzed.

Incoming call: Four Heavenly King, Phoebe.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

Phoebe?

That was unexpected.

Tapping the answer button, he brought the phone to his ear.

"Johan, there's a meeting tomorrow at the League Headquarters. Do you need a private flight arranged?"

Ah—so it was that kind of call.

"Yeah, that'd be great," he replied.

After all, the meeting was about Team Magma and Team Aqua.

They couldn't afford to take things lightly.

Just as he was about to hang up—

His phone vibrated again.

New call: Professor Sycamore, Kalos Region.

Johan frowned slightly.

What was Sycamore calling about?

He picked up.

"Johan, I'm planning to hold a Researcher Conference soon. Do you think you'll be available?"

Ah—Mega Evolution again.

It was still the hottest research topic in the world.

Johan exhaled slowly.

Looks like things weren't going to slow down anytime soon.
